{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_019Y5mAL75FmH36ZUBBRAkY8","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→# Netsmart Technologies — myEvolv Certified Edition Product Research\n     2→\n     3→Researched: 2026-02-16\n     4→Developer website: https://www.ntst.com/\n     5→\n     6→## Overview\n     7→\n     8→Netsmart Technologies is a major healthcare IT company headquartered in Overland Park, Kansas, that develops and sells health information technology for electronic health records (EHRs) and health information exchanges (HIEs) serving the **behavioral health, human services, and post-acute care** markets. The company is the largest HIT vendor specifically focused on these sectors, serving over 500,000 users in more than 24,000 organizations across all 50 states, and impacting more than 25 million individuals nationwide. Netsmart has approximately 2,500 employees and estimated annual revenue of approximately $500-550M (with ~$250M EBITDA reported in 2024).\n     9→\n    10→**History:** The company's technology roots trace back to 1968, when Gerald O. Koop and John F. Phillips founded Creative Socio-Medics. Netsmart itself was created in September 1992 as a holding company originally known as Medical Services Corporation. It acquired Creative Socio-Medics in 1994, and went through several name changes before adopting \"Netsmart Technologies\" in February 1996. In August 1996, it held an IPO on NASDAQ under the symbol NTST. By 2005, Netsmart was the largest IT provider in the behavioral health sector, with revenues of approximately $50M. By 2011, the company had grown to 600 employees and $110M in revenue.\n    11→\n    12→**Ownership:** Netsmart has passed through multiple private equity hands:\n    13→- **2006**: Bought out by Bessemer Venture Partners and Insight Venture Partners for $115M (taken private)\n    14→- **2010**: Acquired by Genstar Capital from Bessemer/Insight\n    15→- **2016**: Bought by GI Partners and Allscripts for $950M; Allscripts merged its homecare software business into Netsmart\n    16→- **2018**: GI Partners and TA Associates acquired Allscripts' stake; Netsmart has been owned by GI Partners and TA Associates since\n    17→- **2024**: Owners explored a sale potentially valuing Netsmart at $5 billion (including debt), working with Goldman Sachs and William Blair; as of early 2026, no completed sale has been publicly confirmed\n    18→\n    19→**Key Acquisitions:**\n    20→- 1994: Creative Socio-Medics (founding technology)\n    21→- 2005: CMHC Systems of Ohio (community mental health centers, $18M) -- largest competitor at the time\n    22→- 2005: ContinuedLearning LLC and Addiction Management Systems\n    23→- 2011: Sequest Technologies (child/family services and substance use treatment)\n    24→- 2016: HealthMEDX (long-term care EHR)\n    25→- 2016: Allscripts homecare business (merged as part of GI Partners acquisition)\n    26→- 2017: DeVero (home health and hospice technology)\n    27→- 2019: McBee (250-employee healthcare consulting firm)\n    28→- 2020: Quality In Real Time (QIRT) (healthcare IT, New York)\n    29→- 2022: TheraOffice (physical therapy EMR/practice management, Chicago area)\n    30→- 2023: Netalytics (addiction treatment solutions, including Methasoft)\n    31→\n    32→Sources:\n    33→- https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netsmart_Technologies\n    34→- https://www.gipartners.com/private-equity/portfolio/netsmart\n    35→- https://finance.yahoo.com/news/exclusive-netsmarts-owners-explore-sale-190517581.html\n    36→- https://www.ntst.com/company/news/news-release-netsmart-announces-strategic-investment-from-ta-associates-and-gi-partners\n    37→- https://tracxn.com/d/acquisitions/acquisitions-by-netsmart-technologies/__aITm9Rhb_TM1btwHXti92s4aRTOM7oicQE_IWvFevTQ\n    38→- https://growjo.com/company/Netsmart_Technologies\n    39→\n    40→## Product: myEvolv Certified Edition\n    41→\n    42→CHPL ID: 11131\n    43→CHPL Product Number: 15.04.04.2816.myEv.11.02.1.221227\n    44→Version: 11.0\n    45→Certification Date: 2022-12-27\n    46→Certification Body: Drummond Group LLC\n    47→\n    48→### What It Is\n    49→\n    50→myEvolv is Netsmart's **behavioral health and human services EHR** -- a fully web-based, ONC-certified electronic health record with comprehensive clinical, financial, and operational features designed specifically for organizations serving specialized populations. Unlike myAvatar (Netsmart's other behavioral health EHR targeting larger psychiatric/addiction organizations), myEvolv focuses on the broader human services ecosystem:\n    51→\n    52→- **Behavioral Health** (community mental health, outpatient behavioral health)\n    53→- **Child and Family Services** (foster care, child welfare, family preservation, early intervention)\n    54→- **Intellectual and Developmental Disabilities (IDD)**\n    55→- **Autism Services** (including Applied Behavior Analysis programs)\n    56→- **Addiction Treatment** (including Medication-Assisted Treatment/MAT)\n    57→- **Certified Community Behavioral Health Clinics (CCBHCs)**\n    58→- **Community-Based Outpatient Providers**\n    59→- **Foster Care**\n    60→- **Juvenile Justice Systems**\n    61→- **Social Services**\n    62→\n    63→myEvolv is a cloud-based SaaS platform that is accessible from any browser and also has iOS and Android mobile apps. The certified edition (version 11.0) carries a broad ONC certification covering 38 criteria, including clinical data management (a)(1)-(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14); transitions of care (b)(1)-(b)(3); EHI export (b)(10)-(b)(11); clinical quality measures (c)(1)-(c)(3); privacy/security (d)(1)-(d)(9), (d)(12)-(d)(13); patient portal/VDT (e)(1), (e)(3); public health reporting (f)(1), (f)(2), (f)(5); FHIR API access (g)(2)-(g)(7), (g)(9)-(g)(10); and direct messaging (h)(1).\n    64→\n    65→The system incorporates state-specific clinical records initiatives such as the New York State Clinical Records Initiative (NYSCRI) and the Massachusetts Standardized Documentation Project (MSDP), indicating significant deployments in these states.\n    66→\n    67→Sources:\n    68→- https://www.ntst.com/offerings/myevolv\n    69→- https://www.ehrinpractice.com/netsmart-myevolv.html\n    70→- https://www.emrsystems.net/myevolv-ehr-software/\n    71→- https://www.findemr.com/myevolv\n    72→- https://www.ntst.com/company/news/netsmart-onc-certification-hs-myevolv-myavatar\n    73→\n    74→### Users & Market\n    75→\n    76→myEvolv targets **mid-market and larger behavioral health and human services organizations**. Day-to-day users include:\n    77→\n    78→- **Behavioral health clinicians** (psychologists, therapists, addiction specialists, social workers, ARNPs, psychiatrists) performing clinical documentation and treatment planning\n    79→- **Community-based direct service providers** documenting services in the field via the mobile application\n    80→- **Case managers and care coordinators** tracking client services across programs and providers\n    81→- **Foster care specialists** managing foster parent applications, certifications, and child placements\n    82→- **ABA therapists and autism services staff** (via RethinkBH integration) performing behavioral data collection\n    83→- **Medication management staff** using eMAR and e-prescribing\n    84→- **Billing and administrative staff** managing complex Medicaid, Medicare, and commercial claims\n    85→- **Organizational administrators** configuring workflows and running reports\n    86→\n    87→**Market Position:** Netsmart (across all its products) is recognized as the **#1 behavioral health EHR vendor** according to Black Book Research, which has given Netsmart top rankings for 9+ consecutive years. In 2025, Black Book surveyed 1,308 behavioral health professionals and 152 non-practitioner professionals and Netsmart maintained its top-rated position across key areas including revenue cycle management, clinical documentation, and patient management. Netsmart has received 26+ Black Book awards across behavioral health, geriatrics, long-term care/post-acute care, home health, and hospice.\n    88→\n    89→However, KLAS Research presents a more nuanced picture -- about two-thirds of surveyed Netsmart customers have said they would not buy the same product again, citing development pace and support concerns (per the myUnity research). This suggests an industry-dominant vendor where customers sometimes feel locked in.\n    90→\n    91→**Notable Deployments:**\n    92→- Arrow Child and Family Ministries -- uses myEvolv for comprehensive foster care and child services records\n    93→- Rainbows United -- uses myEvolv for developmental disability and children's services\n    94→- Missouri Behavioral Health Council -- implemented Netsmart CareManager for CCBHC population health management across the state\n    95→- Organizations incorporating NYSCRI (New York) and MSDP (Massachusetts) standardized documentation\n    96→\n    97→**Competitors** in the behavioral health/human services EHR space include: Qualifacts (CareLogic), Core Solutions, Streamline Healthcare Solutions, Welligent, Valant, NextStep Solutions, TherapyNotes, ICANotes, and BestNotes. Larger general EHR vendors (Epic, Oracle/Cerner) also serve behavioral health but with less specialization.\n    98→\n    99→Sources:\n   100→- https://www.ntst.com/offerings/myevolv\n   101→- https://www.newswire.com/news/netsmart-maintains-leadership-in-ehr-pm-satisfaction-among-behavioral-22506367\n   102→- https://www.newswire.com/news/netsmart-maintains-top-ehr-pm-satisfaction-ratings-among-behavioral-22247136\n   103→- https://www.newswire.com/news/behavioral-health-clinicians-administrators-rate-netsmart-as-highest-22032554\n   104→- https://www.ntst.com/resources-and-insights/success-stories/arrow-child-and-family-ministries\n   105→- https://www.ntst.com/resources-and-insights/success-stories/rainbows-united\n   106→- https://klasresearch.com/vendor-ratings/netsmart/61241\n   107→- https://www.grandviewresearch.com/industry-analysis/us-behavioral-health-ehr-market-report\n   108→\n   109→### Modules & Functionality\n   110→\n   111→myEvolv is organized around clinical, financial, and operational workflows. Key modules and capabilities found in vendor materials and review sites:\n   112→\n   113→**Clinical Documentation:**\n   114→- Configurable clinical templates aligned to evidence-based practices\n   115→- Structured assessments and evaluation tools\n   116→- Progress notes with electronic signatures\n   117→- Real-time access to individual records from the office or the field\n   118→- Treatment planning tools for developing comprehensive, individualized treatment plans based on diagnosis, presenting issues, and goals\n   119→- Evidence-based treatment implementation support\n   120→- Clinical decision support\n   121→- Outcome tracking -- monitoring effectiveness of services by individual, staff, program, and agency\n   122→- Immunization tracking\n   123→- Allergy tracking\n   124→- Lab integration\n   125→\n   126→**Medication Management:**\n   127→- Electronic Medication Administration Record (eMAR)\n   128→- E-prescribing (via OrderConnect platform)\n   129→- Medication history display\n   130→- Medication error reduction through centralized data\n   131→\n   132→**Scheduling & Case Management:**\n   133→- Appointment scheduling and management\n   134→- Referral management\n   135→- Case coordination across programs, providers, and communities\n   136→- Milestone views for tracking required tasks (e.g., foster care applications, certifications)\n   137→\n   138→**Billing & Revenue Cycle:**\n   139→- Automatic claim creation from clinical notes and placement/service data\n   140→- Payer rate determination based on coordination of benefits\n   141→- Medicare and state Medicaid billing support (complex multi-payer rules)\n   142→- Electronic claims submission with real-time monitoring\n   143→- Integrated accounts receivable/accounts pending (AR/AP)\n   144→- Financial data exportable to general ledger systems\n   145→- Integrated credit card payments via CardConnect\n   146→\n   147→**Patient/Consumer Engagement:**\n   148→- Patient portal access (certified under (e)(1) and (e)(3) -- view, download, transmit)\n   149→- myHealthPointe consumer engagement software for provider-patient communication\n   150→- Appointment scheduling and reminders via the Netsmart app\n   151→- Lab results access for patients\n   152→- Electronic appointment scheduling and alerts\n   153→\n   154→**Reporting & Analytics:**\n   155→- Standard reports covering client demographics, service utilization, and billing activity\n   156→- Clinical outcome and performance metric reporting\n   157→- Performance-based care model data collection\n   158→- Service effectiveness tracking by individual, staff, program, and agency\n   159→- CCBHC quality measure reporting\n   160→- Clinical quality measures (certified CQMs include CMS2v6, CMS68v6, CMS69v5, CMS117v5, CMS124v5, CMS125v5, CMS137v5, and others)\n   161→\n   162→**Mobile & Offline:**\n   163→- **myEvolv Anywhere** (CarePOV) -- disconnected mobile application for community-based direct service providers\n   164→- Supports documentation in connected or disconnected environments\n   165→- Automatically re-syncs with the EHR once internet connection is restored\n   166→- Manages appointments, documents services, assessments, and tasks\n   167→- Captures session data, data collection, and electronic signatures on tablets\n   168→- Available on iOS and Android\n   169→\n   170→**Specialized Capabilities:**\n   171→- **Foster Care Management**: Data collection for foster parents from application through recruitment, training, and re-certification; milestone tracking for CPR, first aid, foster care parenting courses\n   172→- **ABA/Autism**: Partnership with RethinkBH (RethinkFirst) for integrated Applied Behavior Analysis clinical documentation, scheduling, treatment planning, data collection, and assessment (mobile-enabled)\n   173→- **Medication-Assisted Treatment (MAT)**: Specialized tracking for MAT programs in addiction treatment\n   174→- **CCBHC Support**: Tools meeting key quality measures for Certified Community Behavioral Health Clinics, including a national CCBHC outcomes and reporting data repository developed with the National Council for Mental Wellbeing\n   175→- **Telehealth/Telemedicine**: Direct care delivery capabilities\n   176→- **Document Management**: Storage and management of clinical documents\n   177→\n   178→**Interoperability:**\n   179→- **Netsmart CareConnect** integration engine for cross-provider data exchange\n   180→- HL7 FHIR R4 certified endpoints (Provider Patient Access API, Payer Patient Access API)\n   181→- SMART on FHIR support for patient-facing applications\n   182→- Carequality and HIE connectivity\n   183→- HL7 and ICD-10 compliance\n   184→- Direct messaging (certified (h)(1))\n   185→- Transitions of care document exchange (certified (b)(1)-(b)(3))\n   186→- Public health reporting: immunization registries (f)(1), syndromic surveillance (f)(2), electronic case reporting (f)(5)\n   187→\n   188→Sources:\n   189→- https://www.ntst.com/offerings/myevolv\n   190→- https://www.ntst.com/Offerings/myEvolv-Anywhere\n   191→- https://www.ehrinpractice.com/netsmart-myevolv.html\n   192→- https://www.emrsystems.net/myevolv-ehr-software/\n   193→- https://www.findemr.com/myevolv\n   194→- https://www.ntst.com/solutions/by-community/human-services/child-and-family-services\n   195→- https://www.ntst.com/company/news/news-release-netsmart-rethink-mypath-partnership\n   196→- https://www.ntst.com/solutions/by-capability/consumer-engagement\n   197→- https://careconnect.netsmartcloud.com/\n   198→- https://www.ntst.com/lp/information-sharing\n   199→- https://www.ntst.com/ccbhc\n   200→- https://apps.apple.com/us/app/carepov-myevolv-anywhere-d/id1560400414\n   201→\n   202→### Data & Content\n   203→\n   204→Based on the features described above and the ONC certification criteria, myEvolv manages a substantial breadth of data across behavioral health and human services settings:\n   205→\n   206→**Clinical Data (evidenced by vendor materials and certification criteria):**\n   207→- Patient/client demographics and unified individual records\n   208→- Clinical assessments and structured evaluation data\n   209→- Treatment plans (individualized, evidence-based, with goals, diagnoses, and presenting issues)\n   210→- Progress notes and clinical documentation\n   211→- Medications (prescriptions, eMAR, administration records, e-prescribing data)\n   212→- Allergies and allergy intolerances\n   213→- Diagnoses and problem lists\n   214→- Immunization records\n   215→- Lab results and diagnostic reports (implied by clinical data criteria (a)(1)-(a)(3))\n   216→- Vital signs\n   217→- Procedures\n   218→- Clinical decision support data and alerts\n   219→- Outcome measures and goal achievement metrics\n   220→- Service delivery records and utilization data\n   221→\n   222→**Behavioral Health / Human Services-Specific Data:**\n   223→- Behavioral health assessments and screening tools\n   224→- Substance use treatment records including MAT program tracking\n   225→- Applied Behavior Analysis (ABA) data (via RethinkBH integration) -- behavioral data collection, treatment plans, session data\n   226→- Foster care records -- foster parent applications, recruitment/training records, certification tracking, milestone completion\n   227→- Child welfare documentation including service plans tied to treatment plans\n   228→- IDD/autism services records\n   229→- Case management records and case coordination data\n   230→- CCBHC quality measures and outcomes data\n   231→\n   232→**Administrative/Financial Data:**\n   233→- Billing records and claims data (Medicare, Medicaid, commercial, multi-payer)\n   234→- Accounts receivable and accounts pending records\n   235→- Payment records including credit card transactions\n   236→- Insurance/eligibility and coordination of benefits data\n   237→- Scheduling and appointment records\n   238→- Staff assignment and workload data\n   239→- Referral records\n   240→- Financial analytics and reporting data\n   241→- General ledger export data\n   242→\n   243→**Regulatory/Compliance Data:**\n   244→- Clinical quality measures (CQMs) data\n   245→- Public health reporting data -- immunization registries, syndromic surveillance, electronic case reporting\n   246→- Audit trails and access logs\n   247→- HIPAA compliance records\n   248→- Transitions of care documents (C-CDAs)\n   249→- Electronic health information (EHI) export data (certified (b)(10)-(b)(11))\n   250→\n   251→**Communication/Engagement Data:**\n   252→- Patient portal data (view, download, transmit per (e)(1) and (e)(3) certification)\n   253→- Consumer engagement communications (myHealthPointe)\n   254→- Secure messaging between providers (CareConnect Inbox)\n   255→- Telehealth session records\n   256→- Appointment reminders and alerts\n   257→- Care coordination communications across programs and providers\n   258→\n   259→**Mobile/Offline Data:**\n   260→- myEvolv Anywhere session data (captured in disconnected mode, synced later)\n   261→- Electronic signatures captured on tablets\n   262→- Field-based service documentation\n   263→\n   264→**Interoperability Data:**\n   265→- FHIR R4 API data (certified for (g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10))\n   266→- C-CDA documents for transitions of care\n   267→- HIE/Carequality-exchanged data\n   268→- Direct messaging communications\n   269→\n   270→The vendor website and materials do not explicitly detail some data types that may exist in the system -- for instance, crisis intervention records, safety planning documents, behavioral incident reports, substance use screening instruments (e.g., AUDIT, DAST), PHQ-9/GAD-7 depression/anxiety screening scores, or detailed group therapy session notes. These are standard in behavioral health EHRs and are very likely supported through myEvolv's configurable templates, but they were not specifically highlighted in the materials reviewed.\n   271→\n   272→Sources:\n   273→- https://www.ntst.com/offerings/myevolv\n   274→- https://www.emrsystems.net/myevolv-ehr-software/\n   275→- https://www.findemr.com/myevolv\n   276→- https://www.ehrinpractice.com/netsmart-myevolv.html\n   277→- https://www.ntst.com/lp/certifications\n   278→\n   279→### User Reviews & Feedback\n   280→\n   281→**Overall Ratings:**\n   282→- Capterra: ~3.0 out of 5 stars\n   283→- EMRSystems: 4.0 out of 5 stars (4 reviews)\n   284→- Black Book Research: #1 ranked behavioral health EHR for 9+ consecutive years\n   285→\n   286→**Commonly Cited Strengths:**\n   287→- Built specifically for behavioral health -- covers progress notes, treatment planning, and Medicaid billing well\n   288→- High configurability for varied organizational workflows and diverse funding requirements\n   289→- Custom templates and streamlined documentation\n   290→- Smooth navigation and easy client data input\n   291→- Clear medication history display supporting safer prescribing\n   292→- Integrated clinical-to-billing workflows reducing manual data entry\n   293→- Multi-platform accessibility (web, mobile)\n   294→- System flexibility for managing diverse programs and funding streams\n   295→- 100% usability testing success rate and 93% satisfaction rate cited by vendor\n   296→- Long-term users report the software evolves well to meet industry changes\n   297→\n   298→**Commonly Cited Weaknesses:**\n   299→- **Implementation**: Described as \"slow, cumbersome\" with \"no one owning the process\" on Netsmart's end; poor project management; data conversion during implementation missed important information requiring manual re-entry\n   300→- **Reporting**: No \"canned\" reports for commonly needed tasks; each report must be individually crafted and cannot be shared between users; reports do not export to Excel well\n   301→- **Customer Support**: Described as \"very poor\" with staff \"not trained properly\"; slow response times; inadequate training and support materials post-go-live\n   302→- **Billing Compliance**: Difficulty implementing billing regulations; The company is the largest HIT vendor specifically focused on these sectors, serving over 500,000 users in more than 24,000 organizations across all 50 states, and impacting more than 25 million individuals nationwide. Netsmart has approximately 2,500 employees and estimated annual revenue of approximately $500-550M (with ~$250M EBITDA reported in 2024).\n\n**History:** The company's technology roots trace back to 1968, when Gerald O. Koop and John F. Phillips founded Creative Socio-Medics. Netsmart itself was created in September 1992 as a holding company originally known as Medical Services Corporation. It acquired Creative Socio-Medics in 1994, and went through several name changes before adopting \"Netsmart Technologies\" in February 1996. In August 1996, it held an IPO on NASDAQ under the symbol NTST. By 2005, Netsmart was the largest IT provider in the behavioral health sector, with revenues of approximately $50M. By 2011, the company had grown to 600 employees and $110M in revenue.\n\n**Ownership:** Netsmart has passed through multiple private equity hands:\n- **2006**: Bought out by Bessemer Venture Partners and Insight Venture Partners for $115M (taken private)\n- **2010**: Acquired by Genstar Capital from Bessemer/Insight\n- **2016**: Bought by GI Partners and Allscripts for $950M; Allscripts merged its homecare software business into Netsmart\n- **2018**: GI Partners and TA Associates acquired Allscripts' stake; Netsmart has been owned by GI Partners and TA Associates since\n- **2024**: Owners explored a sale potentially valuing Netsmart at $5 billion (including debt), working with Goldman Sachs and William Blair; as of early 2026, no completed sale has been publicly confirmed\n\n**Key Acquisitions:**\n- 1994: Creative Socio-Medics (founding technology)\n- 2005: CMHC Systems of Ohio (community mental health centers, $18M) -- largest competitor at the time\n- 2005: ContinuedLearning LLC and Addiction Management Systems\n- 2011: Sequest Technologies (child/family services and substance use treatment)\n- 2016: HealthMEDX (long-term care EHR)\n- 2016: Allscripts homecare business (merged as part of GI Partners acquisition)\n- 2017: DeVero (home health and hospice technology)\n- 2019: McBee (250-employee healthcare consulting firm)\n- 2020: Quality In Real Time (QIRT) (healthcare IT, New York)\n- 2022: TheraOffice (physical therapy EMR/practice management, Chicago area)\n- 2023: Netalytics (addiction treatment solutions, including Methasoft)\n\nSources:\n- https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netsmart_Technologies\n- https://www.gipartners.com/private-equity/portfolio/netsmart\n- https://finance.yahoo.com/news/exclusive-netsmarts-owners-explore-sale-190517581.html\n- https://www.ntst.com/company/news/news-release-netsmart-announces-strategic-investment-from-ta-associates-and-gi-partners\n- https://tracxn.com/d/acquisitions/acquisitions-by-netsmart-technologies/__aITm9Rhb_TM1btwHXti92s4aRTOM7oicQE_IWvFevTQ\n- https://growjo.com/company/Netsmart_Technologies\n\n## Product: myEvolv Certified Edition\n\nCHPL ID: 11131\nCHPL Product Number: 15.04.04.2816.myEv.11.02.1.221227\nVersion: 11.0\nCertification Date: 2022-12-27\nCertification Body: Drummond Group LLC\n\n### What It Is\n\nmyEvolv is Netsmart's **behavioral health and human services EHR** -- a fully web-based, ONC-certified electronic health record with comprehensive clinical, financial, and operational features designed specifically for organizations serving specialized populations. Unlike myAvatar (Netsmart's other behavioral health EHR targeting larger psychiatric/addiction organizations), myEvolv focuses on the broader human services ecosystem:\n\n- **Behavioral Health** (community mental health, outpatient behavioral health)\n- **Child and Family Services** (foster care, child welfare, family preservation, early intervention)\n- **Intellectual and Developmental Disabilities (IDD)**\n- **Autism Services** (including Applied Behavior Analysis programs)\n- **Addiction Treatment** (including Medication-Assisted Treatment/MAT)\n- **Certified Community Behavioral Health Clinics (CCBHCs)**\n- **Community-Based Outpatient Providers**\n- **Foster Care**\n- **Juvenile Justice Systems**\n- **Social Services**\n\nmyEvolv is a cloud-based SaaS platform that is accessible from any browser and also has iOS and Android mobile apps. The certified edition (version 11.0) carries a broad ONC certification covering 38 criteria, including clinical data management (a)(1)-(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14); transitions of care (b)(1)-(b)(3); EHI export (b)(10)-(b)(11); clinical quality measures (c)(1)-(c)(3); privacy/security (d)(1)-(d)(9), (d)(12)-(d)(13); patient portal/VDT (e)(1), (e)(3); public health reporting (f)(1), (f)(2), (f)(5); FHIR API access (g)(2)-(g)(7), (g)(9)-(g)(10); and direct messaging (h)(1).\n\nThe system incorporates state-specific clinical records initiatives such as the New York State Clinical Records Initiative (NYSCRI) and the Massachusetts Standardized Documentation Project (MSDP), indicating significant deployments in these states.\n\nSources:\n- https://www.ntst.com/offerings/myevolv\n- https://www.ehrinpractice.com/netsmart-myevolv.html\n- https://www.emrsystems.net/myevolv-ehr-software/\n- https://www.findemr.com/myevolv\n- https://www.ntst.com/company/news/netsmart-onc-certification-hs-myevolv-myavatar\n\n### Users & Market\n\nmyEvolv targets **mid-market and larger behavioral health and human services organizations**. Day-to-day users include:\n\n- **Behavioral health clinicians** (psychologists, therapists, addiction specialists, social workers, ARNPs, psychiatrists) performing clinical documentation and treatment planning\n- **Community-based direct service providers** documenting services in the field via the mobile application\n- **Case managers and care coordinators** tracking client services across programs and providers\n- **Foster care specialists** managing foster parent applications, certifications, and child placements\n- **ABA therapists and autism services staff** (via RethinkBH integration) performing behavioral data collection\n- **Medication management staff** using eMAR and e-prescribing\n- **Billing and administrative staff** managing complex Medicaid, Medicare, and commercial claims\n- **Organizational administrators** configuring workflows and running reports\n\n**Market Position:** Netsmart (across all its products) is recognized as the **#1 behavioral health EHR vendor** according to Black Book Research, which has given Netsmart top rankings for 9+ consecutive years. In 2025, Black Book surveyed 1,308 behavioral health professionals and 152 non-practitioner professionals and Netsmart maintained its top-rated position across key areas including revenue cycle management, clinical documentation, and patient management. Netsmart has received 26+ Black Book awards across behavioral health, geriatrics, long-term care/post-acute care, home health, and hospice.\n\nHowever, KLAS Research presents a more nuanced picture -- about two-thirds of surveyed Netsmart customers have said they would not buy the same product again, citing development pace and support concerns (per the myUnity research). This suggests an industry-dominant vendor where customers sometimes feel locked in.\n\n**Notable Deployments:**\n- Arrow Child and Family Ministries -- uses myEvolv for comprehensive foster care and child services records\n- Rainbows United -- uses myEvolv for developmental disability and children's services\n- Missouri Behavioral Health Council -- implemented Netsmart CareManager for CCBHC population health management across the state\n- Organizations incorporating NYSCRI (New York) and MSDP (Massachusetts) standardized documentation\n\n**Competitors** in the behavioral health/human services EHR space include: Qualifacts (CareLogic), Core Solutions, Streamline Healthcare Solutions, Welligent, Valant, NextStep Solutions, TherapyNotes, ICANotes, and BestNotes. Larger general EHR vendors (Epic, Oracle/Cerner) also serve behavioral health but with less specialization.\n\nSources:\n- https://www.ntst.com/offerings/myevolv\n- https://www.newswire.com/news/netsmart-maintains-leadership-in-ehr-pm-satisfaction-among-behavioral-22506367\n- https://www.newswire.com/news/netsmart-maintains-top-ehr-pm-satisfaction-ratings-among-behavioral-22247136\n- https://www.newswire.com/news/behavioral-health-clinicians-administrators-rate-netsmart-as-highest-22032554\n- https://www.ntst.com/resources-and-insights/success-stories/arrow-child-and-family-ministries\n- https://www.ntst.com/resources-and-insights/success-stories/rainbows-united\n- https://klasresearch.com/vendor-ratings/netsmart/61241\n- https://www.grandviewresearch.com/industry-analysis/us-behavioral-health-ehr-market-report\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nmyEvolv is organized around clinical, financial, and operational workflows. Key modules and capabilities found in vendor materials and review sites:\n\n**Clinical Documentation:**\n- Configurable clinical templates aligned to evidence-based practices\n- Structured assessments and evaluation tools\n- Progress notes with electronic signatures\n- Real-time access to individual records from the office or the field\n- Treatment planning tools for developing comprehensive, individualized treatment plans based on diagnosis, presenting issues, and goals\n- Evidence-based treatment implementation support\n- Clinical decision support\n- Outcome tracking -- monitoring effectiveness of services by individual, staff, program, and agency\n- Immunization tracking\n- Allergy tracking\n- Lab integration\n\n**Medication Management:**\n- Electronic Medication Administration Record (eMAR)\n- E-prescribing (via OrderConnect platform)\n- Medication history display\n- Medication error reduction through centralized data\n\n**Scheduling & Case Management:**\n- Appointment scheduling and management\n- Referral management\n- Case coordination across programs, providers, and communities\n- Milestone views for tracking required tasks (e.g., foster care applications, certifications)\n\n**Billing & Revenue Cycle:**\n- Automatic claim creation from clinical notes and placement/service data\n- Payer rate determination based on coordination of benefits\n- Medicare and state Medicaid billing support (complex multi-payer rules)\n- Electronic claims submission with real-time monitoring\n- Integrated accounts receivable/accounts pending (AR/AP)\n- Financial data exportable to general ledger systems\n- Integrated credit card payments via CardConnect\n\n**Patient/Consumer Engagement:**\n- Patient portal access (certified under (e)(1) and (e)(3) -- view, download, transmit)\n- myHealthPointe consumer engagement software for provider-patient communication\n- Appointment scheduling and reminders via the Netsmart app\n- Lab results access for patients\n- Electronic appointment scheduling and alerts\n\n**Reporting & Analytics:**\n- Standard reports covering client demographics, service utilization, and billing activity\n- Clinical outcome and performance metric reporting\n- Performance-based care model data collection\n- Service effectiveness tracking by individual, staff, program, and agency\n- CCBHC quality measure reporting\n- Clinical quality measures (certified CQMs include CMS2v6, CMS68v6, CMS69v5, CMS117v5, CMS124v5, CMS125v5, CMS137v5, and others)\n\n**Mobile & Offline:**\n- **myEvolv Anywhere** (CarePOV) -- disconnected mobile application for community-based direct service providers\n- Supports documentation in connected or disconnected environments\n- Automatically re-syncs with the EHR once internet connection is restored\n- Manages appointments, documents services, assessments, and tasks\n- Captures session data, data collection, and electronic signatures on tablets\n- Available on iOS and Android\n\n**Specialized Capabilities:**\n- **Foster Care Management**: Data collection for foster parents from application through recruitment, training, and re-certification; milestone tracking for CPR, first aid, foster care parenting courses\n- **ABA/Autism**: Partnership with RethinkBH (RethinkFirst) for integrated Applied Behavior Analysis clinical documentation, scheduling, treatment planning, data collection, and assessment (mobile-enabled)\n- **Medication-Assisted Treatment (MAT)**: Specialized tracking for MAT programs in addiction treatment\n- **CCBHC Support**: Tools meeting key quality measures for Certified Community Behavioral Health Clinics, including a national CCBHC outcomes and reporting data repository developed with the National Council for Mental Wellbeing\n- **Telehealth/Telemedicine**: Direct care delivery capabilities\n- **Document Management**: Storage and management of clinical documents\n\n**Interoperability:**\n- **Netsmart CareConnect** integration engine for cross-provider data exchange\n- HL7 FHIR R4 certified endpoints (Provider Patient Access API, Payer Patient Access API)\n- SMART on FHIR support for patient-facing applications\n- Carequality and HIE connectivity\n- HL7 and ICD-10 compliance\n- Direct messaging (certified (h)(1))\n- Transitions of care document exchange (certified (b)(1)-(b)(3))\n- Public health reporting: immunization registries (f)(1), syndromic surveillance (f)(2), electronic case reporting (f)(5)\n\nSources:\n- https://www.ntst.com/offerings/myevolv\n- breadth of data across behavioral health and human services settings:\n\n**Clinical Data (evidenced by vendor materials and certification criteria):**\n- Patient/client demographics and unified metrics\n- Service delivery records and utilization data\n\n**Behavioral Health / Human Services-Specific Data:**\n- Behavioral health assessments and screening tools\n- Substance use treatment records\n- Case management records and case coordination data\n- CCBHC quality measures and outcomes data\n\n**Administrative/Financial Data:**\n- Billing records and claims data (Medicare, Medicaid, documents for transitions of care\n- HIE/Carequality-exchanged data\n- Direct messaging communications\n\nThe vendor website and materials do not explicitly detail some data types that may exist in poor project management; data conversion during implementation missed important information requiring manual re-entry\n- **Reporting**: No \"canned\" reports for commonly needed tasks; each report must be individually crafted and cannot be shared between users; reports do not export to Excel well\n- **Customer Support**: Described as \"very poor\" with staff \"not trained properly\"; slow response times; inadequate training and support materials post-go-live\n- **Billing Compliance**: Difficulty implementing billing regulations; help for the financial/billing side described as inadequate\n- **System Performance**: Some reports of slowness and crashes; UI described as \"clunky\" with navigation complaints\n- **Training**: Insufficient training materials and guidance after implementation\n\nSources:\n- https://www.capterra.com/p/209717/myEvolv/reviews/\n- https://www.softwareadvice.com/medical/netsmart-evolv-profile/reviews/\n- https://www.trustradius.com/products/netsmart-myevolv/reviews\n- https://www.emrsystems.net/myevolv-ehr-software/\n- https://www.newswire.com/news/netsmart-maintains-leadership-in-ehr-pm-satisfaction-among-behavioral-22506367\n\n### Netsmart Product Ecosystem\n\nmyEvolv fits within the broader **Netsmart CareFabric** platform, which is described as \"a tightly woven framework of innovative clinical and business solutions and services that supports integrated, coordinated delivery of health services across the spectrum of care.\" Key products in the ecosystem:\n\n| Product | Focus Area | Description |\n|---------|-----------|-------------|\n| **myAvatar** | Behavioral Health (Enterprise) | Larger-scale behavioral health EHR for psychiatric hospitals, substance abuse providers, methadone clinics, CMHCs, managed care, and state mental health systems. 20+ modules. To view the specific system provided forms, tables, and associated data, please see myEvolv All EHI Export Data Dictionary Crosswalk.xlsx.\nConfiguration\nNetsmart delivers a preconfigured template within myEvolv. This template contains a list of all forms and events potentially entered onto a patient’s chart, which can in turn be exported via the All EHI Export tool for reasons of allowing the agency to seamlessly export EHI data.\nTo access the template:\nNavigate to Taskbar > System Maintenance > Application Maintenance > Utilities\nLocate Enable Restart of EHI Exports Jobs and click Run:\nThis ensures the EHI Export job is set to run daily in the system.\nNext, locate Populate EHI Export Print Bundle and click Run:\nThis automatically adds any qualifying events and forms to the EHI Export Print Bundle Template presently missing, such as agency defined forms and events and newly delivered system events provided by Netsmart that have been added into the system after the last time this utility was executed.\nTo enable the Full EHI Export Print Bundle Template:\nNavigate to Agency Setup > Other Setups > Printing Bundle Template > Template:\nClick on the Select Template and select Full EHI Export:\nSelect Is Active and click Save:\nSuccessfully generated All EHI Export File(s) are deposited into an external directory. This directory must be configured prior to generating any EHI Export files, otherwise the files will not be deposited anywhere upon completion. To configure this:\nNavigate to Setup > System > EvolvCS System Setup > System Settings/Preferences\nLocate the System Preference tab.\nEnter the preferred location to the EHI Export Output Directory field.\nClick Save.\nNote: For self-hosted agencies, the EHI Output Directory will be a directory of their choosing. For Netsmart-hosted agencies, this is the location of their NTST-provided SFTP site. To obtain access to this SFTP site, the agency must first open a support case with the Plexus Hosting team.Note: For self-hosted agencies, the EHI Output Directory will be a directory of their choosing. For Netsmart-hosted agencies, this is the location of their NTST-provided SFTP site. To obtain access to this SFTP site, the agency must first open a support case with the Plexus Hosting team.\nNote:\nFor self-hosted agencies, the EHI Output Directory will be a directory of their choosing.\nFor Netsmart-hosted agencies, this is the location of their NTST-provided SFTP site. To obtain access to this SFTP site, the agency must first open a support case with the Plexus Hosting team.\nNote:\nFor self-hosted agencies, the EHI Output Directory will be a directory of their choosing.\nFor Netsmart-hosted agencies, this is the location of their NTST-provided SFTP site. To obtain access to this SFTP site, the agency must first open a support case with the Plexus Hosting team.\nUsage\nThe All EHI Export feature leverages the Print Bundle tool to generate output files based on parameters of the agency’s choosing. To generate output:\nNavigate to Reports > All EHI Exports > All EHI Exports > EHI Export\nSelect Add New and then Bulk Print Bundle.\nOn the Print Bundle Template field, select Full EHI Export.\nAt this point the end-user can determine the parameters they wish to use to filter their export criteria, such as returning all clients or only specific client(s).\nAn example is below:\nClick Save to start the EHI Export process. This can take some time to run, depending on the amount of data myEvolv must return.\nOnce started, the Export Status can be viewed when opening the job:\nOnce a job completes, the Export Status says Completed:\nObtaining EHI Export Files\nThe EHI Export file outputs data in JSON format. The JSON file(s) are placed into a .ZIP file and deposited into a directory. End-Users must then access that directory to obtain their zip file(s).\nCompleted jobs will deposit the .ZIP file into the external folder location entered into the EHI Export Output Directory configuration. At this point there will be one or more .ZIP files with one or more JSON files within. An example is below:\nUnderstanding the EHI Export\nAn EHI Export produces a zip file that contains a series of JSON files. The name of the zip file contains the date the file was generated in a form of “EHI_Export_123120230900.zip” which would say that this export was generated on December 31st, 2023, at 9AM.\nThe JSON file(s) included contain a counter that is appended to the name of each file, which themselves are segregated by file size. The final counter on the file name indicates the order of the files. For example, “Bulk_Bundle_ID_1.json” represents the first file in an export, while “Bulk_Bundle_ID_2.json” would represent the second. The ‘ID’ is a GUID that is a unique identifier for the export.\nWithin each JSON file there are a series of JSON arrays. A single array contains a single record’s worth of data. For example, a single allergy for a single client. This record would correspond to a form within myEvolv, which can be identified using the “x_form_code” property within each JSON array. This value matches the Form Code for a given form (example: Micromedex/Truven Allergy) within myEvolv. The records are sorted by event type.  A single event type may span multiple files, and a given client’s data may also span multiple files. Individual JSON files are size-limited.\nThe “people_id” property is used to identify which person a given record belongs to within the JSON array. Every record contains a people_id. Beyond these two fields, other common and important fields are:\nkey_value: The unique identifier for a record (example: the identifier for a single allergy for a single client)\nevent_definition_id: An additional identifier which identifies the type of event it is, similar to x_form_code\nactual_date: Start Date for an event (not applicable to non-events, such as Demographics)\nend_date: End Date for an event (not applicable to non-events, such as Demographics)\nGenerally speaking, the property name within the JSON corresponds to the column name, which itself corresponds to a field on a form. For example, if a column severity_id field is set to be the ‘Allergic Reaction Severity’ field on the Allergies form, then the property name in the JSON would be “severity_id” and the value will contain the actual severity.\nThe list below contains common data types and how they would appear in the JSON:\nString/freetext: Displays the value containing the entered free-text on the form.\nDate and DateTime: Displays in YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS format. Example, 2023-12-31T09:00:00 represents 12/31/2023 at 9:00 AM.\nForeign Key/Lookup Table Fields: Displayed in two properties:\nThe column name contains the unique identifier for the field, which is a GUID, when populated. For example, site_providing_service have a value of “d6f3b512-2d90-456a-8db7-9ce5f578f273”.\nColumn name + _desc will contain the description for the populated value.  For example, site_providing_service_desc having a value of “Main Street Facility”.\nAdditionally, the following properties/objects are added for the following field types, when present:\nAttachments/Images/Pictures:\nColumn name + _base64 contains the base 64 encoded version of the attachment.\nColumn name + _mimeType contains the type of attachment.\nSignatures:\nColumn name + _sig contains the base 64 encoded version of the signature.\nColum name + _mimeType contains the file type.\nProgress Notes:\nConsolidated into an array (there could be 0-to-many) with the following properties:\nProgress_note_id (unique ID for the note)\nPN_[progress_note_id] will contain the content of the note\nPN_progress_note_author – The ID for the author of the note\nPN_staff_duration – The duration entered on the note\nPN_date_modified – Modified date of the note\nPN_is_main_note – 1/0 value for if this was the main note (important for multi-note scenarios)\nPN_date_entered – Date the note was entered\nSubforms:\nIf the form contained subform(s) that were populated, each subform is represented in an array inside of the parent form. Each row of that subform would be an individual array entry. The naming patterns for the properties are consistent with the parent form as called out above. For example, if an Allergy form has the Reactions/Symptoms subform on it, and two rows are populated for two reactions, then the parent Allergy array would contain an array for the subform (identifiable by x_form_code) that contains two entries (one for each row on the subform/each reaction).\nA unique ID for each subform row is output in the pattern of SF_[ID]_count. The ID will be a GUID that is the unique ID for the row, and the count represents a numeric counter of the subform row.\nTests/Assessments:\nThese are output in the same pattern as subforms.\nThe name of the assessment is output in a “test_json_name” property.\nEach test contains a list of questions and their answers, with the following properties:\ntest_json_name – Name of the assessment\ntest_setup_details_id – ID for the field definition\ntest_setup_details_answers_id – ID for the answer (there will be multiple for multi-selects)\ndate_value – Entered value for date fields\nnumeric_value – Entered value for numeric fields\nscrnval_id – Entered value for lookup fields\npicklist_value – Description for lookup fields\nnarrative – Entered value for freetext fields\nis_checked – 0 or 1 for if a checkbox field is selected\ndetails_type_code – Type code for the field\ntest_setup_answers_caption – Label for the field\ntest_setup_answers_order – Ordering on the assessment\nInterpreting a JSON Example\nThere are two types of files which might be present in the JSON output tied to an output:\nEvent-Based: Events are date-based items entered onto a patient record. These consist of the majority of records found in the EHI Export. Examples of Events include Activities, Tests/Assessments, Medications, Allergies, Consents, Treatment Plans, etc. An event-based output always contains an event_definition_id.\nForm-Based: Forms contain non-date-based information entered onto a patient record. The most common example is the “Personal Demographics” form found on all people records. These are static forms, which are updated as-needed by end-users. Form-based outputs do not contain an event_definition_id, as they do not belong to an event; they are instead a standalone form embedded directly into a formset member.\nIn a further effort to assist users interpreting the export file, here is an example on how one could locate a person, an encounter, a note and a document, in an actual EHI Export JSON.\nA single JSON array from a larger JSON file is provided below. This comes from an internal Netsmart myEvolv test system.\nColor coding has been imbued to each critical data element, so that one can reference these values against the myEvolv All EHI Export Data Dictionary Crosswalk.xlsx to more easily identify and categorize each JSON message.\nPerson = people_id\nEncounter = key_value\nNote = Progress_note_id (and companioning data sub-elements)\nNote: Progress Notes are optional and as such are not present on all encounters.\nDocument = attached_document + attached_document_MimeType\nNote: the name of this data column can vary.\nEvent-Based JSON Example\nBelow is an Event-Based JSON example, where  the “encounter” is interpreted as a myEvolv Event. This client would likely have multiple JSONs of various “encounters”, where each individual JSON array represents a unique encounter. Event-Based encounters are the most common in the EHI JSON output.\nThe following displays one Encounter for a specific person, a Physical Characteristics entry.\nEvents can be identified by referencing the event_definition_id and event_definition_id_desc. This value can be compared to the “Events” and “Events + Columns” tabs of the myEvolv ALL EHI Export Data Dictionary Crosswalk.xlsx to determine the Event Category, Forms, and other information useful false,\n\"is_draft\": false\n}\n]\n}\n],\nSQL Queries\nThe companion file “myEvolv ALL EHI Export Queries.sql” is provided alongside this documentation. An agency can execute these SELECT queries to retrieve similar data found in the Data Dictionary Crosswalk, inclusive of the events and forms found in their myEvolv system. This is useful for obtaining user-defined events and form data which the official Data Dictionary Crosswalk cannot provide.\nThe file contains four queries, which correspond to the four tabs found in the “myEvolv All EHI Export Data Dictionary Crosswalk.xlsx” file:\nEvents: Lists all people-based events which qualify for EHI Export.\nEvents + Columns: A more granular list of the \"Events\" tab, where each field/column on the provided event are provided. Useful for agencies who wish to reference field-by-field data granularity on an event-by-event basis.\nNon-Events: Lists all non-event people-based forms which qualify for EHI Export. A common example is \"Demographics\" (PERSONAL INFORMATION).\nNon-Events + Columns: A more granular list of the \"System Non-Events\" tab, where each field/column on the provided form are provided.\nNetsmart-hosted agencies can execute these scripts in one of two ways:\nIf the agency has a replicated report server, these can be executed directly against the report server.\nAlternatively, agencies can create custom report(s) in NX Custom Reporting and insert these queries into their NX Custom report views.\nSelf-Hosted agencies can execute these scripts against their local myEvolv SQL Database.\nNote: While changes to the queries can freely be made by agencies, support cases will not be reviewed if the queries contain changes to the original iterations provided in the Netsmart-provided file.\nGlossary of Terms\nIn order to assist organizations in interpreting the information here, Netsmart is providing a glossary of terms for those commonly used event-based field descriptions.\nField Name\nDescription\nevent_log_id\nPrimary Key that will be replicated in the extension table\npeople_id\nEvent entry for people\nEvent entries will either have this or the group_profile_id (Profile event) field.  If neither is filled, then it is an incident entry.\nevent_definition_id\nWhere the event is setup or defined (event_definition)\nbelongs_to_event\nevent_log_id (circular reference) if this event was entered within another event.  For example, entering a new client is an event.  If their benefit assignment is also recorded on the initial enrollment, the new client event entry would be filled in this field.  An example in this document is used when we insert event-based sub form Other Activities – B2E (event_log)\nShould be view only on a form and is often labeled ‘entered with’\ncreated_by_event\nEvent automatically created as a result of entering an initial event type\nactual_date\nActual date or start date\ncompleted\nFor multi-day events, indicates completed date\nend_date\nDate the event ended.  For single day events, will be equal to the actual date or actual date + duration entered in this table\nFor events setup as multi-day (see events setups) will be the end date entered and should be available on the form used\ngroup_profile_id\nIf the event was entered for a profile as opposed for a person (group_profile)\napproved_date\nDate event was approved by supervisor for those events that allow supervisory approval\ngroup_event_id\nFor those events that are entered in the groups or family case module this being filled identifies the record that is provided to the group and not the entry made for those who attended. (event_log)\nupdate_log_id\nLinks to the update_log table, which contains details about data entry (time, information updated, who updated)\ngroups_id\nEvent entry for group event\nstaff_id\nStaff that completed the event\nservice_track_event_id\nIf event is provided for a client, this is the event that represents the client’s enrollment into the agency (event_log)\nprogram_enrollment_event_id\nThe event entry that represents the clients program enrollment when the event is a service (event_log)\nlocked_by\nThe user that submits the event for supervisor approval when allowed (staff)\ndate_locked\nDate event was submitted for approval\napproved_by\nStaff supervisor that approved the event (staff)\nis_locked\nEvent was submitted for approval\nprogram_providing_service\nWhen the event is a service, the program that provided the service. (program_info)\nsite_providing_service\nWhen the event is a service, the service facility that provided the service. (group_profile)\ngeneric_remarks\nGeneric remarks that are optionally on most event forms.\nis_client_involved\nTypically used on service entry forms only indicating if client was there or not when receiving the service\nduration\nDuration of the event.  Used mostly for services.  When entered with the actual date for a non-multi-day event, will be used with the actual date to calculate the end_date\nservice_location_facility\nTypically entered with services identifying the actual location it was provided (group_profile)\nactivity_type_id\nTypically entered with services to further define the service (activity_type)\nactual_location\nTypically entered with services to identify a generic location of where the service was provided (location)\nencounter_with_id\nTypically entered with services to identify a generic value representing who the service was provided with (encounter_with)\nunits_id\nIf entry requires a unit of the site providing service, typically enrollments into facilities.  (units)\ndate_entered\nSystem date the event was entered\nThis would be also in the audit log as the first entry but is entered here for ease of use and reporting\nevent_agency_id\nAgency this event was entered for, only applied to multi-agency systems. (Agency)\nuser_entered\nUser that entered this data (Staff)\napproval_sent_to\nWhen an event is supplied to a supervisor for approval but is overridden with a specific approval entry by the user, such as on incidents and plans. (Staff)\ndiagnoses_id\nEntry of this field will ensure that this code will be supplied with the bill; otherwise the client’s primary and secondary diagnoses entered on file will be supplied (diagnoses)\nis_deleted\nWhen an event (service, program enrollment, benefit assignment, etc) that has been used for billing is deleted, it is flagged as deleted with this field but not removed from the database\nis_billed\nUsed to denote if the service has been billed for\nattached_document\nUsed to link a file to the event record.  Files of any type (.doc, .xls, .jpg, etc.) can be linked, and the file is then stored in the data base\nfrom_events2do_id\nIf the event was scheduled, this is the link to the scheduling record in events2do table\nservice_authorization_details_id\nIf agency is required to use Service Authorizations, this field is used to link a service to the Service Authorization\nagency_program_service_link_id\nAssociates event with agency and program placement for billing services\nprogram_unit_id\nShows which Program Unit the service is billed under, if required\nType Code Legend\nThis legend contains a comprehensive list of data types that could be included in the extracted {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_012BA9f696qbkX9gqvC1HinP","type":"tool_result","content":"     1→EHI Export: myEvolv Event and Table Documentation Last Updated: 11/04/2024EHI Export: myEvolv Event and Table Documentation Last Updated: 11/04/2024\n     2→EHI Export: myEvolv Event and Table Documentation\n     3→Last Updated: 11/04/2024\n     4→EHI Export: myEvolv Event and Table Documentation\n     5→Last Updated: 11/04/2024\n     6→Contents\n     7→Overview3\n     8→Configuration3\n     9→Usage6\n    10→Obtaining EHI Export Files7\n    11→Understanding the EHI Export8\n    12→Interpreting a JSON Example11\n    13→Event-Based JSON Example11\n    14→Form-Based JSON Example15\n    15→SQL Queries25\n    16→Glossary of Terms26\n    17→Type Code Legend29\n    18→Overview\n    19→This document is a companion guide to the myEvolv All EHI Export Data Dictionary and will assist in interpreting the data created via the myEvolv EHI Extract tool. To view the specific system provided forms, tables, and associated data, please see myEvolv All EHI Export Data Dictionary Crosswalk.xlsx.\n    20→Configuration\n    21→Netsmart delivers a preconfigured template within myEvolv. This template contains a list of all forms and events potentially entered onto a patient’s chart, which can in turn be exported via the All EHI Export tool for reasons of allowing the agency to seamlessly export EHI data.\n    22→To access the template:\n    23→Navigate to Taskbar > System Maintenance > Application Maintenance > Utilities\n    24→Locate Enable Restart of EHI Exports Jobs and click Run:\n    25→This ensures the EHI Export job is set to run daily in the system.\n    26→Next, locate Populate EHI Export Print Bundle and click Run:\n    27→This automatically adds any qualifying events and forms to the EHI Export Print Bundle Template presently missing, such as agency defined forms and events and newly delivered system events provided by Netsmart that have been added into the system after the last time this utility was executed.\n    28→To enable the Full EHI Export Print Bundle Template:\n    29→Navigate to Agency Setup > Other Setups > Printing Bundle Template > Template:\n    30→Click on the Select Template and select Full EHI Export:\n    31→Select Is Active and click Save:\n    32→Successfully generated All EHI Export File(s) are deposited into an external directory. This directory must be configured prior to generating any EHI Export files, otherwise the files will not be deposited anywhere upon completion. To configure this:\n    33→Navigate to Setup > System > EvolvCS System Setup > System Settings/Preferences\n    34→Locate the System Preference tab.\n    35→Enter the preferred location to the EHI Export Output Directory field.\n    36→Click Save.\n    37→Note: For self-hosted agencies, the EHI Output Directory will be a directory of their choosing. For Netsmart-hosted agencies, this is the location of their NTST-provided SFTP site. To obtain access to this SFTP site, the agency must first open a support case with the Plexus Hosting team.Note: For self-hosted agencies, the EHI Output Directory will be a directory of their choosing. For Netsmart-hosted agencies, this is the location of their NTST-provided SFTP site. To obtain access to this SFTP site, the agency must first open a support case with the Plexus Hosting team.\n    38→Note:\n    39→For self-hosted agencies, the EHI Output Directory will be a directory of their choosing.\n    40→For Netsmart-hosted agencies, this is the location of their NTST-provided SFTP site. To obtain access to this SFTP site, the agency must first open a support case with the Plexus Hosting team.\n    41→Note:\n    42→For self-hosted agencies, the EHI Output Directory will be a directory of their choosing.\n    43→For Netsmart-hosted agencies, this is the location of their NTST-provided SFTP site. To obtain access to this SFTP site, the agency must first open a support case with the Plexus Hosting team.\n    44→Usage\n    45→The All EHI Export feature leverages the Print Bundle tool to generate output files based on parameters of the agency’s choosing. To generate output:\n    46→Navigate to Reports > All EHI Exports > All EHI Exports > EHI Export\n    47→Select Add New and then Bulk Print Bundle.\n    48→On the Print Bundle Template field, select Full EHI Export.\n    49→At this point the end-user can determine the parameters they wish to use to filter their export criteria, such as returning all clients or only specific client(s).\n    50→An example is below:\n    51→Click Save to start the EHI Export process. This can take some time to run, depending on the amount of data myEvolv must return.\n    52→Once started, the Export Status can be viewed when opening the job:\n    53→Once a job completes, the Export Status says Completed:\n    54→Obtaining EHI Export Files\n    55→The EHI Export file outputs data in JSON format. The JSON file(s) are placed into a .ZIP file and deposited into a directory. End-Users must then access that directory to obtain their zip file(s).\n    56→Completed jobs will deposit the .ZIP file into the external folder location entered into the EHI Export Output Directory configuration. At this point there will be one or more .ZIP files with one or more JSON files within. An example is below:\n    57→Understanding the EHI Export\n    58→An EHI Export produces a zip file that contains a series of JSON files. The name of the zip file contains the date the file was generated in a form of “EHI_Export_123120230900.zip” which would say that this export was generated on December 31st, 2023, at 9AM.\n    59→The JSON file(s) included contain a counter that is appended to the name of each file, which themselves are segregated by file size. The final counter on the file name indicates the order of the files. For example, “Bulk_Bundle_ID_1.json” represents the first file in an export, while “Bulk_Bundle_ID_2.json” would represent the second. The ‘ID’ is a GUID that is a unique identifier for the export.\n    60→Within each JSON file there are a series of JSON arrays. A single array contains a single record’s worth of data. For example, a single allergy for a single client. This record would correspond to a form within myEvolv, which can be identified using the “x_form_code” property within each JSON array. This value matches the Form Code for a given form (example: Micromedex/Truven Allergy) within myEvolv. The records are sorted by event type.  A single event type may span multiple files, and a given client’s data may also span multiple files. Individual JSON files are size-limited.\n    61→The “people_id” property is used to identify which person a given record belongs to within the JSON array. Every record contains a people_id. Beyond these two fields, other common and important fields are:\n    62→key_value: The unique identifier for a record (example: the identifier for a single allergy for a single client)\n    63→event_definition_id: An additional identifier which identifies the type of event it is, similar to x_form_code\n    64→actual_date: Start Date for an event (not applicable to non-events, such as Demographics)\n    65→end_date: End Date for an event (not applicable to non-events, such as Demographics)\n    66→Generally speaking, the property name within the JSON corresponds to the column name, which itself corresponds to a field on a form. For example, if a column severity_id field is set to be the ‘Allergic Reaction Severity’ field on the Allergies form, then the property name in the JSON would be “severity_id” and the value will contain the actual severity.\n    67→The list below contains common data types and how they would appear in the JSON:\n    68→String/freetext: Displays the value containing the entered free-text on the form.\n    69→Date and DateTime: Displays in YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS format. Example, 2023-12-31T09:00:00 represents 12/31/2023 at 9:00 AM.\n    70→Foreign Key/Lookup Table Fields: Displayed in two properties:\n    71→The column name contains the unique identifier for the field, which is a GUID, when populated. For example, site_providing_service have a value of “d6f3b512-2d90-456a-8db7-9ce5f578f273”.\n    72→Column name + _desc will contain the description for the populated value.  For example, site_providing_service_desc having a value of “Main Street Facility”.\n    73→Additionally, the following properties/objects are added for the following field types, when present:\n    74→Attachments/Images/Pictures:\n    75→Column name + _base64 contains the base 64 encoded version of the attachment.\n    76→Column name + _mimeType contains the type of attachment.\n    77→Signatures:\n    78→Column name + _sig contains the base 64 encoded version of the signature.\n    79→Colum name + _mimeType contains the file type.\n    80→Progress Notes:\n    81→Consolidated into an array (there could be 0-to-many) with the following properties:\n    82→Progress_note_id (unique ID for the note)\n    83→PN_[progress_note_id] will contain the content of the note\n    84→PN_progress_note_author – The ID for the author of the note\n    85→PN_staff_duration – The duration entered on the note\n    86→PN_date_modified – Modified date of the note\n    87→PN_is_main_note – 1/0 value for if this was the main note (important for multi-note scenarios)\n    88→PN_date_entered – Date the note was entered\n    89→Subforms:\n    90→If the form contained subform(s) that were populated, each subform is represented in an array inside of the parent form. Each row of that subform would be an individual array entry. The naming patterns for the properties are consistent with the parent form as called out above. For example, if an Allergy form has the Reactions/Symptoms subform on it, and two rows are populated for two reactions, then the parent Allergy array would contain an array for the subform (identifiable by x_form_code) that contains two entries (one for each row on the subform/each reaction).\n    91→A unique ID for each subform row is output in the pattern of SF_[ID]_count. The ID will be a GUID that is the unique ID for the row, and the count represents a numeric counter of the subform row.\n    92→Tests/Assessments:\n    93→These are output in the same pattern as subforms.\n    94→The name of the assessment is output in a “test_json_name” property.\n    95→Each test contains a list of questions and their answers, with the following properties:\n    96→test_json_name – Name of the assessment\n    97→test_setup_details_id – ID for the field definition\n    98→test_setup_details_answers_id – ID for the answer (there will be multiple for multi-selects)\n    99→date_value – Entered value for date fields\n   100→numeric_value – Entered value for numeric fields\n   101→scrnval_id – Entered value for lookup fields\n   102→picklist_value – Description for lookup fields\n   103→narrative – Entered value for freetext fields\n   104→is_checked – 0 or 1 for if a checkbox field is selected\n   105→details_type_code – Type code for the field\n   106→test_setup_answers_caption – Label for the field\n   107→test_setup_answers_order – Ordering on the assessment\n   108→Interpreting a JSON Example\n   109→There are two types of files which might be present in the JSON output tied to an output:\n   110→Event-Based: Events are date-based items entered onto a patient record. These consist of the majority of records found in the EHI Export. Examples of Events include Activities, Tests/Assessments, Medications, Allergies, Consents, Treatment Plans, etc. An event-based output always contains an event_definition_id.\n   111→Form-Based: Forms contain non-date-based information entered onto a patient record. The most common example is the “Personal Demographics” form found on all people records. These are static forms, which are updated as-needed by end-users. Form-based outputs do not contain an event_definition_id, as they do not belong to an event; they are instead a standalone form embedded directly into a formset member.\n   112→In a further effort to assist users interpreting the export file, here is an example on how one could locate a person, an encounter, a note and a document, in an actual EHI Export JSON.\n   113→A single JSON array from a larger JSON file is provided below. This comes from an internal Netsmart myEvolv test system.\n   114→Color coding has been imbued to each critical data element, so that one can reference these values against the myEvolv All EHI Export Data Dictionary Crosswalk.xlsx to more easily identify and categorize each JSON message.\n   115→Person = people_id\n   116→Encounter = key_value\n   117→Note = Progress_note_id (and companioning data sub-elements)\n   118→Note: Progress Notes are optional and as such are not present on all encounters.\n   119→Document = attached_document + attached_document_MimeType\n   120→Note: the name of this data column can vary.\n   121→Event-Based JSON Example\n   122→Below is an Event-Based JSON example, where  the “encounter” is interpreted as a myEvolv Event. This client would likely have multiple JSONs of various “encounters”, where each individual JSON array represents a unique encounter. Event-Based encounters are the most common in the EHI JSON output.\n   123→The following displays one Encounter for a specific person, a Physical Characteristics entry.\n   124→Events can be identified by referencing the event_definition_id and event_definition_id_desc. An agency can execute these SELECT queries to retrieve similar data found in the Data Dictionary Crosswalk, inclusive of the events and forms found in their myEvolv system. This is useful for obtaining user-defined events and form data which the official Data Dictionary Crosswalk cannot provide.\n   510→The file contains four queries, which correspond to the four tabs found in the “myEvolv All EHI Export Data Dictionary Crosswalk.xlsx” file:\n   511→Events: Lists all people-based events which qualify for EHI Export.\n   512→Events + Columns: A more granular list of the \"Events\" tab, where each field/column on the provided event are provided. Useful for agencies who wish to reference field-by-field data granularity on an event-by-event basis.\n   513→Non-Events: Lists all non-event people-based forms which qualify for EHI Export. A common example is \"Demographics\" (PERSONAL INFORMATION).\n   514→Non-Events + Columns: A more granular list of the \"System Non-Events\" tab, where each field/column on the provided form are provided.\n   515→Netsmart-hosted agencies can execute these scripts in one of two ways:\n   516→If the agency has a replicated report server, these can be executed directly against the report server.\n   517→Alternatively, agencies can create custom report(s) in NX Custom Reporting and insert these queries into their NX Custom report views.\n   518→Self-Hosted agencies can execute these scripts against their local myEvolv SQL Database.\n   519→Note: While changes to the queries can freely be made by agencies, support cases will not be reviewed if the queries contain changes to the original iterations provided in the Netsmart-provided file.\n   520→Glossary of Terms\n   521→In order to assist organizations in interpreting the information here, Netsmart is providing a glossary of terms for those commonly used event-based field descriptions.\n   522→Field Name\n   523→Description\n   524→event_log_id\n   525→Primary Key that will be replicated in the extension table\n   526→people_id\n   527→Event entry for people\n   528→Event entries will either have this or the group_profile_id (Profile event) field.  If neither is filled, then it is an incident entry.\n   529→event_definition_id\n   530→Where the event is setup or defined (event_definition)\n   531→belongs_to_event\n   532→event_log_id (circular reference) if this event was entered within another event.  For example, entering a new client is an event.  If their benefit assignment is also recorded on the initial enrollment, the new client event entry would be filled in this field.  An example in this document is used when we insert event-based sub form Other Activities – B2E (event_log)\n   533→Should be view only on a form and is often labeled ‘entered with’\n   534→created_by_event\n   535→Event automatically created as a result of entering an initial event type\n   536→actual_date\n   537→Actual date or start date\n   538→completed\n   539→For multi-day events, indicates completed date\n   540→end_date\n   541→Date the event ended.  For single day events, will be equal to the actual date or actual date + duration entered in this table\n   542→For events setup as multi-day (see events setups) will be the end date entered and should be available on the form used\n   543→group_profile_id\n   544→If the event was entered for a profile as opposed for a person (group_profile)\n   545→approved_date\n   546→Date event was approved by supervisor for those events that allow supervisory approval\n   547→group_event_id\n   548→For those events that are entered in the groups or family case module this being filled identifies the record that is provided to the group and not the entry made for those who attended. (event_log)\n   549→update_log_id\n   550→Links to the update_log table, which contains details about data entry (time, information updated, who updated)\n   551→groups_id\n   552→Event entry for group event\n   553→staff_id\n   554→Staff that completed the event\n   555→service_track_event_id\n   556→If event is provided for a client, this is the event that represents the client’s enrollment into the agency (event_log)\n   557→program_enrollment_event_id\n   558→The event entry that represents the clients program enrollment when the event is a service (event_log)\n   559→locked_by\n   560→The user that submits the event for supervisor approval when allowed (staff)\n   561→date_locked\n   562→Date event was submitted for approval\n   563→approved_by\n   564→Staff supervisor that approved the event (staff)\n   565→is_locked\n   566→Event was submitted for approval\n   567→program_providing_service\n   568→When the event is a service, the program that provided the service. (program_info)\n   569→site_providing_service\n   570→When the event is a service, the service facility that provided the service. (group_profile)\n   571→generic_remarks\n   572→Generic remarks that are optionally on most event forms.\n   573→is_client_involved\n   574→Typically used on service entry forms only indicating if client was there or not when receiving the service\n   575→duration\n   576→Duration of the event.  Used mostly for services.  When entered with the actual date for a non-multi-day event, will be used with the actual date to calculate the end_date\n   577→service_location_facility\n   578→Typically entered with services identifying the actual location it was provided (group_profile)\n   579→activity_type_id\n   580→Typically entered with services to further define the service (activity_type)\n   581→actual_location\n   582→Typically entered with services to identify a generic location of where the service was provided (location)\n   583→encounter_with_id\n   584→Typically entered with services to identify a generic value representing who the service was provided with (encounter_with)\n   585→units_id\n   586→If entry requires a unit of the site providing service, typically enrollments into facilities.  (units)\n   587→date_entered\n   588→System date the event was entered\n   589→This would be also in the audit log as the first entry but is entered here for ease of use and reporting\n   590→event_agency_id\n   591→Agency this event was entered for, only applied to multi-agency systems. (Agency)\n   592→user_entered\n   593→User that entered this data (Staff)\n   594→approval_sent_to\n   595→When an event is supplied to a supervisor for approval but is overridden with a specific approval entry by the user, such as on incidents and plans. (Staff)\n   596→diagnoses_id\n   597→Entry of this field will ensure that this code will be supplied with the bill; otherwise the client’s primary and secondary diagnoses entered on file will be supplied (diagnoses)\n   598→is_deleted\n   599→When an event (service, program enrollment, benefit assignment, etc) that has been used for billing is deleted, it is flagged as deleted with this field but not removed from the database\n   600→is_billed\n   601→Used to denote if the service has been billed for\n   602→attached_document\n   603→Used to link a file to the event record.  Files of any type (.doc, .xls, .jpg, etc.) can be linked, and the file is then stored in the data base\n   604→from_events2do_id\n   605→If the event was scheduled, this is the link to the scheduling record in events2do table\n   606→service_authorization_details_id\n   607→If agency is required to use Service Authorizations, this field is used to link a service to the Service Authorization\n   608→agency_program_service_link_id\n   609→Associates event with agency and program placement for billing services\n   610→program_unit_id\n   611→Shows which Program Unit the service is billed under, if required\n   612→Type Code Legend\n   613→This legend contains a comprehensive list of data types that could be included in the extracted documentation.\n   614→Type_Code\n   615→Datatype_name\n
