To view the specific system provided forms, tables, and associated data, please see myEvolv All EHI Export Data Dictionary Crosswalk.xlsx.\nConfiguration\nNetsmart delivers a preconfigured template within myEvolv. This template contains a list of all forms and events potentially entered onto a patient’s chart, which can in turn be exported via the All EHI Export tool for reasons of allowing the agency to seamlessly export EHI data.\nTo access the template:\nNavigate to Taskbar > System Maintenance > Application Maintenance > Utilities\nLocate Enable Restart of EHI Exports Jobs and click Run:\nThis ensures the EHI Export job is set to run daily in the system.\nNext, locate Populate EHI Export Print Bundle and click Run:\nThis automatically adds any qualifying events and forms to the EHI Export Print Bundle Template presently missing, such as agency defined forms and events and newly delivered system events provided by Netsmart that have been added into the system after the last time this utility was executed.\nTo enable the Full EHI Export Print Bundle Template:\nNavigate to Agency Setup > Other Setups > Printing Bundle Template > Template:\nClick on the Select Template and select Full EHI Export:\nSelect Is Active and click Save:\nSuccessfully generated All EHI Export File(s) are deposited into an external directory. This directory must be configured prior to generating any EHI Export files, otherwise the files will not be deposited anywhere upon completion. To configure this:\nNavigate to Setup > System > EvolvCS System Setup > System Settings/Preferences\nLocate the System Preference tab.\nEnter the preferred location to the EHI Export Output Directory field.\nClick Save.\nNote: For self-hosted agencies, the EHI Output Directory will be a directory of their choosing. For Netsmart-hosted agencies, this is the location of their NTST-provided SFTP site. To obtain access to this SFTP site, the agency must first open a support case with the Plexus Hosting team.Note: For self-hosted agencies, the EHI Output Directory will be a directory of their choosing. For Netsmart-hosted agencies, this is the location of their NTST-provided SFTP site. To obtain access to this SFTP site, the agency must first open a support case with the Plexus Hosting team.\nNote:\nFor self-hosted agencies, the EHI Output Directory will be a directory of their choosing.\nFor Netsmart-hosted agencies, this is the location of their NTST-provided SFTP site. To obtain access to this SFTP site, the agency must first open a support case with the Plexus Hosting team.\nNote:\nFor self-hosted agencies, the EHI Output Directory will be a directory of their choosing.\nFor Netsmart-hosted agencies, this is the location of their NTST-provided SFTP site. To obtain access to this SFTP site, the agency must first open a support case with the Plexus Hosting team.\nUsage\nThe All EHI Export feature leverages the Print Bundle tool to generate output files based on parameters of the agency’s choosing. To generate output:\nNavigate to Reports > All EHI Exports > All EHI Exports > EHI Export\nSelect Add New and then Bulk Print Bundle.\nOn the Print Bundle Template field, select Full EHI Export.\nAt this point the end-user can determine the parameters they wish to use to filter their export criteria, such as returning all clients or only specific client(s).\nAn example is below:\nClick Save to start the EHI Export process. This can take some time to run, depending on the amount of data myEvolv must return.\nOnce started, the Export Status can be viewed when opening the job:\nOnce a job completes, the Export Status says Completed:\nObtaining EHI Export Files\nThe EHI Export file outputs data in JSON format. The JSON file(s) are placed into a .ZIP file and deposited into a directory. End-Users must then access that directory to obtain their zip file(s).\nCompleted jobs will deposit the .ZIP file into the external folder location entered into the EHI Export Output Directory configuration. At this point there will be one or more .ZIP files with one or more JSON files within. An example is below:\nUnderstanding the EHI Export\nAn EHI Export produces a zip file that contains a series of JSON files. The name of the zip file contains the date the file was generated in a form of “EHI_Export_123120230900.zip” which would say that this export was generated on December 31st, 2023, at 9AM.\nThe JSON file(s) included contain a counter that is appended to the name of each file, which themselves are segregated by file size. The final counter on the file name indicates the order of the files. For example, “Bulk_Bundle_ID_1.json” represents the first file in an export, while “Bulk_Bundle_ID_2.json” would represent the second. The ‘ID’ is a GUID that is a unique identifier for the export.\nWithin each JSON file there are a series of JSON arrays. A single array contains a single record’s worth of data. For example, a single allergy for a single client. This record would correspond to a form within myEvolv, which can be identified using the “x_form_code” property within each JSON array. This value matches the Form Code for a given form (example: Micromedex/Truven Allergy) within myEvolv. The records are sorted by event type.  A single event type may span multiple files, and a given client’s data may also span multiple files. Individual JSON files are size-limited.\nThe “people_id” property is used to identify which person a given record belongs to within the JSON array. Every record contains a people_id. Beyond these two fields, other common and important fields are:\nkey_value: The unique identifier for a record (example: the identifier for a single allergy for a single client)\nevent_definition_id: An additional identifier which identifies the type of event it is, similar to x_form_code\nactual_date: Start Date for an event (not applicable to non-events, such as Demographics)\nend_date: End Date for an event (not applicable to non-events, such as Demographics)\nGenerally speaking, the property name within the JSON corresponds to the column name, which itself corresponds to a field on a form. For example, if a column severity_id field is set to be the ‘Allergic Reaction Severity’ field on the Allergies form, then the property name in the JSON would be “severity_id” and the value will contain the actual severity.\nThe list below contains common data types and how they would appear in the JSON:\nString/freetext: Displays the value containing the entered free-text on the form.\nDate and DateTime: Displays in YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM:SS format. Example, 2023-12-31T09:00:00 represents 12/31/2023 at 9:00 AM.\nForeign Key/Lookup Table Fields: Displayed in two properties:\nThe column name contains the unique identifier for the field, which is a GUID, when populated. For example, site_providing_service have a value of “d6f3b512-2d90-456a-8db7-9ce5f578f273”.\nColumn name + _desc will contain the description for the populated value.  For example, site_providing_service_desc having a value of “Main Street Facility”.\nAdditionally, the following properties/objects are added for the following field types, when present:\nAttachments/Images/Pictures:\nColumn name + _base64 contains the base 64 encoded version of the attachment.\nColumn name + _mimeType contains the type of attachment.\nSignatures:\nColumn name + _sig contains the base 64 encoded version of the signature.\nColum name + _mimeType contains the file type.\nProgress Notes:\nConsolidated into an array (there could be 0-to-many) with the following properties:\nProgress_note_id (unique ID for the note)\nPN_[progress_note_id] will contain the content of the note\nPN_progress_note_author – The ID for the author of the note\nPN_staff_duration – The duration entered on the note\nPN_date_modified – Modified date of the note\nPN_is_main_note – 1/0 value for if this was the main note (important for multi-note scenarios)\nPN_date_entered – Date the note was entered\nSubforms:\nIf the form contained subform(s) that were populated, each subform is represented in an array inside of the parent form. Each row of that subform would be an individual array entry. The naming patterns for the properties are consistent with the parent form as called out above. For example, if an Allergy form has the Reactions/Symptoms subform on it, and two rows are populated for two reactions, then the parent Allergy array would contain an array for the subform (identifiable by x_form_code) that contains two entries (one for each row on the subform/each reaction).\nA unique ID for each subform row is output in the pattern of SF_[ID]_count. The ID will be a GUID that is the unique ID for the row, and the count represents a numeric counter of the subform row.\nTests/Assessments:\nThese are output in the same pattern as subforms.\nThe name of the assessment is output in a “test_json_name” property.\nEach test contains a list of questions and their answers, with the following properties:\ntest_json_name – Name of the assessment\ntest_setup_details_id – ID for the field definition\ntest_setup_details_answers_id – ID for the answer (there will be multiple for multi-selects)\ndate_value – Entered value for date fields\nnumeric_value – Entered value for numeric fields\nscrnval_id – Entered value for lookup fields\npicklist_value – Description for lookup fields\nnarrative – Entered value for freetext fields\nis_checked – 0 or 1 for if a checkbox field is selected\ndetails_type_code – Type code for the field\ntest_setup_answers_caption – Label for the field\ntest_setup_answers_order – Ordering on the assessment\nInterpreting a JSON Example\nThere are two types of files which might be present in the JSON output tied to an output:\nEvent-Based: Events are date-based items entered onto a patient record. These consist of the majority of records found in the EHI Export. Examples of Events include Activities, Tests/Assessments, Medications, Allergies, Consents, Treatment Plans, etc. An event-based output always contains an event_definition_id.\nForm-Based: Forms contain non-date-based information entered onto a patient record. The most common example is the “Personal Demographics” form found on all people records. These are static forms, which are updated as-needed by end-users. Form-based outputs do not contain an event_definition_id, as they do not belong to an event; they are instead a standalone form embedded directly into a formset member.\nIn a further effort to assist users interpreting the export file, here is an example on how one could locate a person, an encounter, a note and a document, in an actual EHI Export JSON.\nA single JSON array from a larger JSON file is provided below. This comes from an internal Netsmart myEvolv test system.\nColor coding has been imbued to each critical data element, so that one can reference these values against the myEvolv All EHI Export Data Dictionary Crosswalk.xlsx to more easily identify and categorize each JSON message.\nPerson = people_id\nEncounter = key_value\nNote = Progress_note_id (and companioning data sub-elements)\nNote: Progress Notes are optional and as such are not present on all encounters.\nDocument = attached_document + attached_document_MimeType\nNote: the name of this data column can vary.\nEvent-Based JSON Example\nBelow is an Event-Based JSON example, where  the “encounter” is interpreted as a myEvolv Event. This client would likely have multiple JSONs of various “encounters”, where each individual JSON array represents a unique encounter. Event-Based encounters are the most common in the EHI JSON output.\nThe following displays one Encounter for a specific person, a Physical Characteristics entry.\nEvents can be identified by referencing the event_definition_id and event_definition_id_desc. This value can be compared to the “Events” and “Events + Columns” tabs of the myEvolv ALL EHI Export Data Dictionary Crosswalk.xlsx to determine the Event Category, Forms, and other information useful An agency can execute these SELECT queries to retrieve similar data found in the Data Dictionary Crosswalk, inclusive of the events and forms found in their myEvolv system. This is useful for obtaining user-defined events and form data which the official Data Dictionary Crosswalk cannot provide.\nThe file contains four queries, which correspond to the four tabs found in the “myEvolv All EHI Export Data Dictionary Crosswalk.xlsx” file:\nEvents: Lists all people-based events which qualify for EHI Export.\nEvents + Columns: A more granular list of the \"Events\" tab, where each field/column on the provided event are provided. Useful for agencies who wish to reference field-by-field data granularity on an event-by-event basis.\nNon-Events: Lists all non-event people-based forms which qualify for EHI Export. A common example is \"Demographics\" (PERSONAL INFORMATION).\nNon-Events + Columns: A more granular list of the \"System Non-Events\" tab, where each field/column on the provided form are provided.\nNetsmart-hosted agencies can execute these scripts in one of two ways:\nIf the agency has a replicated report server, these can be executed directly against the report server.\nAlternatively, agencies can create custom report(s) in NX Custom Reporting and insert these queries into their NX Custom report views.\nSelf-Hosted agencies can execute these scripts against their local myEvolv SQL Database.\nNote: While changes to the queries can freely be made by agencies, support cases will not be reviewed if the queries contain changes to the original iterations provided in the Netsmart-provided file.\nGlossary of Terms\nIn order to assist organizations in interpreting the information here, Netsmart is providing a glossary of terms for those commonly used event-based field descriptions.\nField Name\nDescription\nevent_log_id\nPrimary Key that will be replicated in the extension table\npeople_id\nEvent entry for people\nEvent entries will either have this or the group_profile_id (Profile event) field.  If neither is filled, then it is an incident entry.\nevent_definition_id\nWhere the event is setup or defined (event_definition)\nbelongs_to_event\nevent_log_id (circular reference) if this event was entered within another event.  For example, entering a new client is an event.  If their benefit assignment is also recorded on the initial enrollment, the new client event entry would be filled in this field.  An example in this document is used when we insert event-based sub form Other Activities – B2E (event_log)\nShould be view only on a form and is often labeled ‘entered with’\ncreated_by_event\nEvent automatically created as a result of entering an initial event type\nactual_date\nActual date or start date\ncompleted\nFor multi-day events, indicates completed date\nend_date\nDate the event ended.  For single day events, will be equal to the actual date or actual date + duration entered in this table\nFor events setup as multi-day (see events setups) will be the end date entered and should be available on the form used\ngroup_profile_id\nIf the event was entered for a profile as opposed for a person (group_profile)\napproved_date\nDate event was approved by supervisor for those events that allow supervisory approval\ngroup_event_id\nFor those events that are entered in the groups or family case module this being filled identifies the record that is provided to the group and not the entry made for those who attended. (event_log)\nupdate_log_id\nLinks to the update_log table, which contains details about data entry (time, information updated, who updated)\ngroups_id\nEvent entry for group event\nstaff_id\nStaff that completed the event\nservice_track_event_id\nIf event is provided for a client, this is the event that represents the client’s enrollment into the agency (event_log)\nprogram_enrollment_event_id\nThe event entry that represents the clients program enrollment when the event is a service (event_log)\nlocked_by\nThe user that submits the event for supervisor approval when allowed (staff)\ndate_locked\nDate event was submitted for approval\napproved_by\nStaff supervisor that approved the event (staff)\nis_locked\nEvent was submitted for approval\nprogram_providing_service\nWhen the event is a service, the program that provided the service. (program_info)\nsite_providing_service\nWhen the event is a service, the service facility that provided the service. (group_profile)\ngeneric_remarks\nGeneric remarks that are optionally on most event forms.\nis_client_involved\nTypically used on service entry forms only indicating if client was there or not when receiving the service\nduration\nDuration of the event.  Used mostly for services.  When entered with the actual date for a non-multi-day event, will be used with the actual date to calculate the end_date\nservice_location_facility\nTypically entered with services identifying the actual location it was provided (group_profile)\nactivity_type_id\nTypically entered with services to further define the service (activity_type)\nactual_location\nTypically entered with services to identify a generic location of where the service was provided (location)\nencounter_with_id\nTypically entered with services to identify a generic value representing who the service was provided with (encounter_with)\nunits_id\nIf entry requires a unit of the site providing service, typically enrollments into facilities.  (units)\ndate_entered\nSystem date the event was entered\nThis would be also in the audit log as the first entry but is entered here for ease of use and reporting\nevent_agency_id\nAgency this event was entered for, only applied to multi-agency systems. (Agency)\nuser_entered\nUser that entered this data (Staff)\napproval_sent_to\nWhen an event is supplied to a supervisor for approval but is overridden with a specific approval entry by the user, such as on incidents and plans. (Staff)\ndiagnoses_id\nEntry of this field will ensure that this code will be supplied with the bill; otherwise the client’s primary and secondary diagnoses entered on file will be supplied (diagnoses)\nis_deleted\nWhen an event (service, program enrollment, benefit assignment, etc) that has been used for billing is deleted, it is flagged as deleted with this field but not removed from the database\nis_billed\nUsed to denote if the service has been billed for\nattached_document\nUsed to link a file to the event record.  Files of any type (.doc, .xls, .jpg, etc.) can be linked, and the file is then stored in the data base\nfrom_events2do_id\nIf the event was scheduled, this is the link to the scheduling record in events2do table\nservice_authorization_details_id\nIf agency is required to use Service Authorizations, this field is used to link a service to the Service Authorization\nagency_program_service_link_id\nAssociates event with agency and program placement for billing services\nprogram_unit_id\nShows which Program Unit the service is billed under, if required\nType Code Legend\nThis legend contains a comprehensive list of data types that could be included in the extracted Clicked the **\"myEvolv®\"** link, which navigated to:\n   ```\n   https://www.ntst.com/lp/certifications/ehi-all-data-myevolv\n   ```\n\n4. **The EHI page** describes the export functionality and contains a prominent \"CLICK HERE\" link to download documentation:\n   ```\n   curl -sL \"https://www.ntst.com/-/media/pdfs/certifications/myevolv-all-ehi-export-documentation_v2.zip\" \\\n     -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0' \\\n     -o myevolv-all-ehi-export-documentation_v2.zip\n   ```\n   This returned a 11.1 MB ZIP file.\n\n5. **Also downloaded the certification status letter** linked from the main certifications page:\n   ```\n   curl -sL \"https://www.ntst.com/-/media/pdfs/certifications/myevolv_certification_status_letter_040425.pdf\" \\\n     -H 'User-Agent: Mozilla/5.0' \\\n     -o myevolv_certification_status_letter_040425.pdf\n   ```\n\n6. **Extracted the ZIP** containing three files:\n   - `(1) myEvolv All EHI Export Companion Guide.docx` (939 KB) — comprehensive user guide\n   - `(2) myEvolv All EHI Export Data Dictionary Crosswalk.xlsx` (11.2 MB) — massive data dictionary with 5 sheets\n   - `(3) myEvolv ALL EHI Export Queries.sql` (6 KB) — SQL queries for agencies to discover their own forms/events\n\n7. **Took a full-page screenshot** of the EHI export page.\n\n## What Was Found\n\nNetsmart provides a dedicated, purpose-built EHI export tool for myEvolv that is clearly designed for (b)(10) compliance. This is **not** a repurposed FHIR API or USCDI export — it is a native data extraction system that exports the full breadth of clinical, administrative, and specialty data stored in myEvolv's SQL Server database.\n\n### Export Mechanism\n\nThe export uses myEvolv's \"Print Bundle\" infrastructure. An administrator configures a \"Full EHI Export\" template that lists all event types and forms eligible for export. The process:\n\n1. Navigate to Reports > All EHI Exports > EHI Export\n2. Select \"Full EHI Export\" template\n3. Choose parameters (all clients or specific clients)\n4. Run the export (asynchronous, can take significant time)\n5. Output is deposited as ZIP files to a configured directory (local path for self-hosted, SFTP for Netsmart-hosted)\n\n### Export Format\n\nThe export produces **JSON files** packaged in ZIP archives. Key characteristics:\n\n- **File naming**: `EHI_Export_MMDDYYYYHHMM.zip` containing `Bulk_Bundle_ID_N.json` files\n- **Structure**: Each JSON file contains arrays of records. Individual records correspond to form entries or events in myEvolv.\n- **Record identification**: Each record has `x_form_code` (form identifier), `people_id` (patient), `key_value` (unique record ID), and for events, `event_definition_id`.\n- **Size-limited**: Individual JSON files are capped at a size limit; a patient's data may span multiple files.\n- **Sorted by event type**: Records are grouped by event type across files.\n\n**Data types in JSON**:\n- Strings, dates (ISO 8601), booleans, numeric values\n- Foreign key fields include both the GUID (`field_id`) and human-readable description (`field_id_desc`) and prompt (`field_id_prompt`)\n- **Attachments/images**: Base64-encoded with MIME type (`field_base64`, `field_mimeType`)\n- **Signatures**: Base64-encoded images\n- **Progress notes**: Nested arrays with note ID, content, author, duration, dates\n- **Subforms**: Nested arrays within parent records (e.g., allergy reactions within an allergy record)\n- **Assessments/tests**: Nested structures with questions, answers, scores, and field type codes\n\n### Data Dictionary\n\nThe XLSX data dictionary crosswalk is remarkably comprehensive:\n\n| Sheet | Rows | Description |\n|-------|------|-------------|\n| Info | 4 | Metadata about the crosswalk |\n| Events | 1,679 | All event definitions (date-based clinical records) |\n| Events + Columns | 115,663 | Field-level detail for all events including subforms and assessments |\n| Non-Events | 250 | Non-event forms (demographics, static records) |\n| Non-Events + Columns | 21,006 | Field-level detail for non-event forms |\n\n**Total: ~138,600 field-level entries** across events and non-events.\n\nThe crosswalk represents a \"freshly installed myEvolv instance\" and does not include user-defined events, which agencies can discover using the provided SQL queries.\n\n### SQL Queries\n\nFour T-SQL queries are provided that can be run against the myEvolv database:\n1. **Events**: Lists all people-based events eligible for EHI export\n2. **Events + Columns**: Field-level detail for each event\n3. **Non-Events**: Lists non-event forms\n4. **Non-Events + Columns**: Field-level detail for non-event forms\n\nThese allow agencies to generate their own crosswalk including custom forms/events.\n\n### Type Code Legend\n\nThe companion guide includes a comprehensive type code legend mapping 50+ data type codes to their SQL types (e.g., FK = Foreign Key/uniqueidentifier, SF = Sub Form, M = Memo/varchar(max), DT = DateTime, etc.).\n\n### Glossary\n\nA detailed glossary defines 35+ common field names found in event-based records (e.g., `event_log_id`, `belongs_to_event`, `service_track_event_id`, `program_providing_service`, etc.) with explanations of their meaning in the myEvolv data model.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\nThe export documentation reveals an **exceptionally broad** coverage of data domains. With 119 event categories and 87 unique database tables, the export encompasses virtually everything myEvolv stores about patients.\n\n**Clearly Covered Domains** (with specific event categories/tables):\n\n| Domain | Evidence |\n|--------|----------|\n| **Demographics** | 250 non-event forms including Personal Information, address, race, ethnicity, language, contact info, identifiers |\n| **Diagnoses** | \"Diagnosis\" event category, `diagnosis_data` table |\n| **Medications** | \"Medication History\", \"Medication Administration\", \"Standing Orders\" categories; `medication_history`, `medication_administered_history`, `standing_orders` tables |\n| **Allergies** | \"Allergies\" category, `people_allergies` table |\n| **Immunizations** | \"Immunizations\" category, `immunization_history` table |\n| **Lab Tests** | \"Lab Tests\" category, `lab_tests` table |\n| **Vital Signs/Physical Characteristics** | \"Physical Characteristics\" category, `physical_characteristics` table; detailed example in companion guide shows height, weight, BMI, BP, pulse, respiration, temperature, SpO2, etc. |\n| **Problems/Needs** | \"Problems/Needs\" category, `problems` table |\n| **Progress Notes** | \"NYSCRI Progress Notes\", \"Care Manager Notes\" categories; nested progress note arrays in export |\n| **Treatment/Service Plans** | \"Treatment/Service Plans for People\", \"Treatment/Service Plans for Profiles\", \"Service Plan Development\", \"Service Plan Addendum\" categories; `service_plan_header`, `service_plan_development`, `service_plan_addendum` tables |\n| **Assessments/Tests** | \"Test/Assessments for People\", \"MSDP Tests and Assessments\", \"NYSCRI Test and Assessments\", \"HCBS Test/Assessments\", \"Family Case Test/Assessment\" — extensive assessment infrastructure |\n| **Consents** | \"Consents\", \"System Consents\" categories, `consents` table |\n| **Incidents** | \"Incidents Header\", \"Incident Medical Exam\", \"Incident Medications\", \"Incident Physical Findings\", \"Incident Restraints\" — 5 separate categories; `incident_header`, `incident_medical_exam`, etc. |\n| **ABA/Autism Services** | \"ABA Data Collection\", \"ABA Service\", \"ABA Session\" categories; `aba_data_collection`, `aba_session` tables |\n| **Substance Use** | \"Substance Use\" category, `substance_use_history` table |\n| **Behavioral Monitoring** | \"Behavioral Monitoring Activities\", \"Behavioral Monitoring Activities - Other\" categories |\n| **Foster Care** | \"Adoption Activities\", \"Placement Disruptions\", \"Placement and Treatment History\", \"Permanency Plan Goals\" categories |\n| **Referrals** | \"Referrals Made\", \"Referrals Made Status\", \"Referrals to Agency\" categories; `referrals_made`, `referrals_made_status`, `referrals_to_agency` tables |\n| **Enrollment/Placement** | \"Program Enrollment\", \"Benefit Assignment\", \"Service Track - Agency Placement\", \"Memberships/Placement in Profiles\" categories |\n| **Billing-Adjacent Data** | \"Authorization Requests\", \"Income Information\", \"Monthly Income\", \"Resources and Liabilities\" categories; `authorization_requests`, `income_history`, `monthly_income`, `resource_liability_data` tables; benefit assignments and program enrollment tracked |\n| **Legal History** | \"Legal History for People\", \"Legal History for Profiles\" categories |\n| **Smoking Status** | \"Smoking Status\" category, `smoking_status` table |\n| **Pregnancy** | \"Pregnancy\", \"Primary Birth Control\" categories |\n| **Employment** | \"Employment History\" category, `employment_history` table |\n| **Marital History** | \"Marital History\" category, `marital_history` table |\n| **Disclosures** | \"Disclosures\" category, `disclosure` table |\n| **Letters/Documents** | \"Letters\", \"Received Documents\" categories |\n| **Evidence-Based Practice** | \"Evidence Based Practice\" category |\n| **Restrictions/Alerts** | \"Restrictions/Alerts\" category, `restrictions` table |\n| **Outreach** | \"Outreach Header\" category |\n| **Worker Assignments** | 4 separate categories for Case, Client, Profiles, Referral assignments |\n| **Public Health** | \"Public Health Activities\", \"Public Health Encounters\", \"Public Health Tests/Assessments\" |\n| **State Reporting** | \"State Reporting Forms\", \"State Reporting Requirements\", \"State Reporting Agency Specific Records\" |\n| **Images/Attachments** | Base64-encoded in export; explicit handling for documents, images, signatures |\n| **E-Signatures** | \"eSignature\" category; base64-encoded signature images in export |\n\n**Notably Present — Specialty/BH-Specific Data**:\n- ABA data collection (interval recording, session data)\n- MST (Multisystemic Therapy) events\n- NYSCRI and MSDP state-specific documentation\n- CCBHC-related assessments\n- Methadone/MAT demographics forms\n- Foster care/adoption activities\n- Incident management with restraints, medications, physical findings\n- HIV status history\n- Behavioral monitoring\n\n**Potential Gaps**:\n- **Billing claims/charges**: While authorization requests, income, benefit assignments, and program enrollment are covered, I do not see explicit \"claims\", \"charges\", \"payments\", or \"billing\" event categories in the export. The certification status letter mentions billing capabilities, and the product has extensive billing/revenue cycle features. The financial data present (authorization requests, income, benefit assignments) is patient-adjacent billing data, but the actual claims/charges/payments/AR data may not be exported. This could be a significant gap given that billing records are part of the designated record set.\n- **Scheduling/appointments**: There is no explicit scheduling/appointment category. While this is borderline EHI (scheduling data is generally operational), service events inherently capture date/time/duration information that documents encounters.\n- **Telehealth session records**: No explicit telehealth category, though telehealth encounters may be captured as regular activities/events.\n- **Group therapy session notes**: \"Group Activities\" and \"Group Activities - Other\" categories exist, which likely cover this.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses a **proprietary JSON format native to myEvolv** — this is not FHIR, C-CDA, or any recognized standard. This is entirely appropriate for a (b)(10) export:\n\n- The JSON structure directly reflects myEvolv's internal data model (events, forms, subforms, assessments)\n- Foreign key fields include both GUIDs and human-readable descriptions, making the data interpretable without access to the source system\n- Binary data (images, documents, signatures) is base64-encoded inline\n- The format is computable and parseable by any JSON-capable tool\n- The data dictionary crosswalk provides the key to interpreting the export structure\n\n**Could a third party reconstruct the patient record?** Yes, largely. The combination of:\n1. The JSON export with `_desc` fields for all foreign keys\n2. The 138,600-row data dictionary crosswalk\n3. The companion guide with worked examples and type code legend\n4. The glossary of common field names\n\n...provides sufficient context to understand what each record represents and how records relate to each other. The `people_id` links records to patients, `belongs_to_event` shows event nesting, and `x_form_code` maps to the crosswalk.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths**:\n- The companion guide is well-written and thorough, with step-by-step configuration instructions, annotated JSON examples, a complete type code legend, and a glossary of terms\n- The data dictionary crosswalk is massive (138K+ rows) and covers the full breadth of the system's forms and events at the field level\n- The SQL queries allow agencies to generate their own crosswalk including custom content\n- The documentation clearly distinguishes event-based vs. form-based records and explains the export structure\n- Worked examples with color-coded annotations show how to interpret the JSON output\n- Last updated November 2024 — recently maintained\n\n**Weaknesses**:\n- The DOCX format for the companion guide is less accessible than HTML or PDF (though the content is high quality)\n- No sample export JSON file is provided as a standalone artifact (examples are embedded in the DOCX)\n- The data dictionary notes that it represents a \"freshly installed\" system and may not match a specific agency's configuration — agencies must run the SQL queries to get their actual data dictionary\n- Relationship documentation between tables is implicit (via foreign keys with `_desc` suffix) rather than explicit (no ERD or relationship diagram)\n- Value sets/code lists are not documented — FK fields show \"Foreign Key ID\" as the type but don't enumerate the possible values\n\n**Overall**: This is significantly above-average EHI export documentation. The level of detail — 138K+ field-level entries, worked examples, SQL queries for customization — demonstrates genuine effort to make the export interpretable.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Granularity**: Field-level documentation with column names, captions, type codes, and form/subform/assessment hierarchy\n- **Data types**: Comprehensive type code legend with 50+ types mapped to SQL data types\n- **Coded fields**: Foreign key fields are identified by type code \"FK\" but value sets are not enumerated\n- **Relationships**: Implicit through foreign key naming conventions and the `belongs_to_event` pattern; no explicit relationship diagram\n- **Versioning**: Document dated 11/04/2024; ZIP filename includes \"_v2\" suggesting at least one prior version\n\n### (b)(10) vs (g)(10) Assessment\n\nThis is a **genuine (b)(10) implementation**. Key evidence:\n\n1. **The export is not FHIR** — it's a native JSON dump of the myEvolv database structure\n2. **Coverage far exceeds USCDI** — includes ABA data, incident management, foster care, legal history, substance use, behavioral monitoring, and dozens of other specialty domains\n3. **The page explicitly distinguishes** EHI export from FHIR APIs: \"If you are working with a Netsmart customer and want to understand different options on integrating with our solutions, please visit our Netsmart Developer Portal for documentation regarding our FHIR API's\"\n4. **119 event categories** vs. the ~20 USCDI data classes — this is clearly a full data export, not a USCDI subset\n5. **Binary data included** — base64-encoded images, documents, and signatures\n\n## Access Summary\n- Registered URL: https://www.ntst.com/lp/certifications\n- Final URL (EHI page): https://www.ntst.com/lp/certifications/ehi-all-data-myevolv\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (curl works for downloads, but browser helps navigate the hub page)\n- Navigation complexity: one_click (from certifications hub to EHI page, then one download link)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\nNone. While this is borderline EHI (scheduling data is generally operational), service events inherently capture date/time/duration information that documents encounters.\n- **Telehealth session records**: No explicit telehealth category, though telehealth encounters may be captured as regular activities/events.\n- **Group therapy session notes**: \"Group Activities\" and \"Group Activities - Other\" categories exist, which likely cover this.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe export uses a **proprietary JSON format native to myEvolv** — this is not FHIR, C-CDA, or any recognized standard. This is entirely appropriate for a (b)(10) export:\n\n- The JSON structure directly reflects myEvolv's internal data model (events, forms, subforms, assessments)\n- Foreign key fields include both GUIDs and human-readable descriptions, making the data interpretable without access to the source system\n- Binary data (images, documents, signatures) is base64-encoded inline\n- The format is computable and parseable by any JSON-capable tool\n- The data dictionary crosswalk provides the key to interpreting the export structure\n\n**Could a third party reconstruct the patient record?** Yes, largely. The combination of:\n1. The JSON export with `_desc` fields for all foreign keys\n2. The 138,600-row data dictionary crosswalk\n3. The companion guide with worked examples and type code legend\n4. The glossary of common field names\n\n...provides sufficient context to understand what each record represents and how records relate to each other. The `people_id` links records to patients, `belongs_to_event` shows event nesting, and `x_form_code` maps to the crosswalk.\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths**:\n- The companion guide is well-written and thorough, with step-by-step configuration instructions, annotated JSON examples, a complete type code legend, and a glossary of terms\n- The data dictionary crosswalk is massive (138K+ rows) and covers the full breadth of the system's forms and events at the field level\n- The SQL queries allow agencies to generate their own crosswalk including custom content\n- The documentation clearly distinguishes event-based vs. form-based records and explains the export structure\n- Worked examples with color-coded annotations show how to interpret the JSON output\n- Last updated November 2024 — recently maintained\n\n**Weaknesses**:\n- The DOCX format for the companion guide is less accessible than HTML or PDF (though the content is high quality)\n- No sample export JSON file is provided as a standalone artifact (examples are embedded in the DOCX)\n- The data dictionary notes that it represents a \"freshly installed\" system and may not match a specific agency's configuration — agencies must run the SQL queries to get their actual data dictionary\n- Relationship documentation between tables is implicit (via foreign keys with `_desc` suffix) rather than explicit (no ERD or relationship diagram)\n- Value sets/code lists are not documented — FK fields show \"Foreign Key ID\" as the type but don't enumerate the possible values\n\n**Overall**: This is significantly above-average EHI export documentation. The level of detail — 138K+ field-level entries, worked examples, SQL queries for customization — demonstrates genuine effort to make the export interpretable.\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n- **Granularity**: Field-level documentation with column names, captions, type codes, and form/subform/assessment hierarchy\n- **Data types**: Comprehensive type code legend with 50+ types mapped to SQL data types\n- **Coded fields**: Foreign key fields are identified by type code \"FK\" but value sets are not enumerated\n- **Relationships**: Implicit through foreign key naming conventions and the `belongs_to_event` pattern; no explicit relationship diagram\n- **Versioning**: Document dated 11/04/2024; ZIP filename includes \"_v2\" suggesting at least one prior version\n\n### (b)(10) vs (g)(10) Assessment\n\nThis is a **genuine (b)(10) implementation**. Key evidence:\n\n1. **The export is not FHIR** — it's a native JSON dump of the myEvolv database structure\n2. **Coverage far exceeds USCDI** — includes ABA data, incident management, foster care, legal history, substance use, behavioral monitoring, and dozens of other specialty domains\n3. **The page explicitly distinguishes** EHI export from FHIR APIs: \"If you are working with a Netsmart customer and want to understand different options on integrating with our solutions, please visit our Netsmart Developer Portal for documentation regarding our FHIR API's\"\n4. **119 event categories** vs. the ~20 USCDI data classes — this is clearly a full data export, not a USCDI subset\n5. **Binary data included** — base64-encoded images, documents, and signatures\n\n## Access Summary\n- Registered URL: https://www.ntst.com/lp/certifications\n- Final URL (EHI page): https://www.ntst.com/lp/certifications/ehi-all-data-myevolv\n- Status: found\n- Required browser: no (curl works for downloads, but browser helps navigate the hub page)\n- Navigation complexity: one_click (from certifications hub to EHI page, then one download link)\n- Anti-bot issues: none\n\n## Obstacles & Dead Ends\nNone.
